27
), (
29
), and (
16
)

(38)

\displaystyle I_{sign}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{B_{R}}\int_{B_{R}}\big[f(v)-f(w)\big]\Big[f_{l,\varepsilon}^{-(1-\zeta)}(v)-f_{l,\varepsilon}^{-(1-\zeta)}(w)\Big]\eta^{2}(v)

\displaystyle\times K^{sym}(v,w)\chi_{f(w)>f(v)>l}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{2(1-\zeta)}{\zeta^{2}}\int_{B_{R}}\int_{B_{R}}\left(f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\frac{\zeta}{2}}(v)-f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\frac{\zeta}{2}}(w)\right)^{2}\eta^{2}(v)

\displaystyle\times K^{sym}(v,w)\chi_{f(w)>f(v)>l}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2(1-\zeta)}{\zeta^{2}}\int_{B_{R}}\int_{B_{R}}f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\zeta}(w)\big(\eta(v)-\eta(w))^{2}

\displaystyle\times K^{sym}(v,w)\chi_{f(w)>f(v)>l}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle-\frac{1-\zeta}{\zeta^{2}}\int_{B_{R}}\int_{B_{R}}\left[\left(f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\frac{\zeta}{2}}\eta\right)(v)-\left(f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\frac{\zeta}{2}}\eta\right)(w)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\times K^{sym}(v,w)\chi_{f(w)>f(v)>l}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq C(\zeta,\Lambda_{0})R^{-2s}\int_{B_{R}}(f-l+\varepsilon)^{\zeta}(w)\chi_{f(w)>l}\,{\rm d}w

\displaystyle-\frac{1-\zeta}{\zeta^{2}}\int_{B_{R}}\int_{B_{R}}\left[\left(f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\frac{\zeta}{2}}\eta\right)(v)-\left(f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\frac{\zeta}{2}}\eta\right)(w)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\times K^{sym}(v,w)\chi_{f(w)>f(v)>l}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v.

This was the good term. Concerning the unsigned term
I_{sym}
, we use (
28
), Young’s inequality, (
30
) and (
16
) so that for any
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)

(39)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle I_{sym}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{B_{R}}\int_{B_{R}}\big[f(v)-f(w)\big]f_{l,\varepsilon}^{-(1-\zeta)}(w)\big[\eta^{2}(v)-\eta^{2}(w)\big]

\displaystyle\times K^{sym}(v,w)\chi_{f(w)>f(v)>l}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\zeta}\int_{B_{R}}\int_{B_{R}}f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\frac{\zeta}{2}}(w)\left[f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\frac{\zeta}{2}}(w)-f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\frac{\zeta}{2}}(v)\right]\left|\eta^{2}(w)-\eta^{2}(v)\right|

\displaystyle\times K^{sym}(v,w)\chi_{f(w)>f(v)>l}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq C(\delta_{0},\zeta)\int_{B_{R}}\int_{B_{R}}f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\zeta}(w)\left|\eta(w)-\eta(v)\right|^{2}K^{sym}(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+\frac{\delta_{0}}{2}\int_{B_{R}}\int_{B_{R}}\max\{\eta^{2}(v),\eta^{2}(w)\}\left[f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\frac{\zeta}{2}}(w)-f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\frac{\zeta}{2}}(v)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\times K^{sym}(v,w)\chi_{f(w)>f(v)>l}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq C(\delta_{0},\zeta)\int_{B_{R}}\int_{B_{R}}f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\zeta}(w)\left|\eta(w)-\eta(v)\right|^{2}K(v,w)\chi_{f>l}(w)\ \,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+\delta_{0}\int_{B_{R}}\int_{B_{R}}f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\zeta}(v)\left|\eta(w)-\eta(v)\right|^{2}K(v,w)\chi_{f>l}(v)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+\delta_{0}\int_{B_{R}}\int_{B_{R}}\left[\left(\eta f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\frac{\zeta}{2}}\right)(w)-\left(f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\frac{\zeta}{2}}\right)(v)\right]^{2}K^{sym}(v,w)\chi_{f(w)>f(v)>l}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq C(\delta_{0},\zeta,\Lambda_{0})R^{-2s}\int_{B_{R}}(f-l+\varepsilon)^{\zeta}(v)\chi_{f>l}(v)\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+\delta_{0}\int_{B_{R}}\int_{B_{R}}\left[\left(f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\frac{\zeta}{2}}\eta\right)(w)-\left(f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\frac{\zeta}{2}}\eta\right)(v)\right]^{2}K^{sym}(v,w)\chi_{f(w)>f(v)>l}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v.

In particular, the last term can be absorbed with the signed term of
I_{sign}
in (
38
) for
\delta_{0}
sufficiently small and for any
\zeta<1
, so that we conclude from (
37
), (
38
), and (
39
)

(40)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{up}^{loc}=I_{sign}+I_{sym}

\displaystyle\leq C(\delta_{0},\zeta,\Lambda_{0})R^{-2s}\int_{B_{R}}(f-l+\varepsilon)^{\zeta}(v)\chi_{f>l}(v)\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle-\frac{1-\zeta}{\zeta^{2}}\int_{B_{R}}\int_{B_{R}}\left[\left(f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\frac{\zeta}{2}}\eta\right)(v)-\left(f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\frac{\zeta}{2}}\eta\right)(w)\right]^{2}K^{sym}(v,w)\chi_{f(w)>f(v)>l}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+\delta_{0}\int_{B_{R}}\int_{B_{R}}\left[\left(f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\frac{\zeta}{2}}\eta\right)(w)-\left(f_{l,\varepsilon}^{\frac{\zeta}{2}}\eta\right)(v)\right]^{2}K^{sym}(v,w)\chi_{f(w)>f(v)>l}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq C(\delta_{0},\zeta,\Lambda_{0})R^{-2s}\int_{B_{R}}(f-l+\varepsilon)^{\zeta}(v)\chi_{f>l}(v)\,{\rm d}v.

Step 1-iv: Bound on the non-locality.

We conclude from (
33
), (
34
), (
35
), (
36
), and (
40
)

(41)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle\left(f,\psi f_{l,\varepsilon}^{-(1-\zeta)}\right)=\mathcal{E}_{up}+\mathcal{E}_{low}+\mathcal{E}_{cross}

\displaystyle\leq C(\zeta,\Lambda_{0})R^{-2s}\int_{B_{R}}(f-l+\varepsilon)^{\zeta}(v)\chi_{f>l}\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle-\int_{B_{R}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{R}}(f-l)_{+}(w)\eta^{2}(v)f_{l,\varepsilon}^{-(1-\zeta)}(v)K(v,w)\chi_{f(v)>l}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+C\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}(\bar{R}-r)^{-2s}\int_{B_{\bar{R}}}f(v)\,{\rm d}v+\frac{\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}}{2}\left(\frac{r}{2}\right)^{-2s}\int_{B_{\frac{r}{2}}}(f-l)_{+}(v)\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+Clr^{-2s}\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}\left|\{f>l\}\cap B_{\frac{r}{2}}\right|.

Note that this estimate holds true for any
(t,x)
.

Step 2: Transport.

We start with the transport operator. We integrate over a torus,

{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y):=\left\{\left(\sqrt{(x_{1}-y_{1})^{2}+(x_{2}-y_{2})^{2}}-R_{2}\right)^{2}+\sum_{i=3}^{d}(x_{i}-y_{i})^{2}=R_{1}^{2}\right\},

with minor radius
R_{1}:=\frac{1}{2}R^{1+2s}
, major radius
R_{2}:=\frac{3}{4}R^{1+2s}
, centred at
y=(\frac{3}{4}R^{1+2s},0,\dots,0)
. Then
{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)\subset B_{2R^{1+2s}}
, and we get

\displaystyle\int_{-R^{2s}}^{0}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}v\cdot\nabla_{x}f(z)\psi_{l}(z)f_{l,\varepsilon}^{-(1-\zeta)}(z)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}v\cdot\nabla_{x}(f-l)_{+}(z)\eta^{2}(z)f_{l,\varepsilon}^{-(1-\zeta)}(z)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}\int_{-R^{2s}}^{0}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}v\cdot\nabla_{x}(f-l)_{-}(z)\xi(v)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\zeta}\int_{-R^{2s}}^{0}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big((f-l)_{+}+\varepsilon\big)^{\zeta}(z)\eta^{2}(z)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t.

The last equality holds due to the divergence theorem applied to the integration in the spatial variable
x
.
Now we use
\left|v\cdot\nabla_{x}\eta\right|\sim R^{-2s}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\zeta}

\displaystyle\int_{-R^{2s}}^{0}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big((f-l)_{+}+\varepsilon\big)^{\zeta}(z)\eta^{2}(z)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\zeta}\int_{-R^{2s}}^{0}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big((f-l)_{+}+\varepsilon\big)^{\zeta}(z)v\cdot\nabla_{x}\eta^{2}(z)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq CR^{-2s}\int_{Q_{R}}\big((f-l)_{+}+\varepsilon\big)^{\zeta}(z)\,{\rm d}z.

We continue with the time derivative. We denote by
t_{1}:=-R^{2s}
and by
t_{2}=0
, so that

\displaystyle\int_{-R^{2s}}^{0}

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\partial_{t}f(z)\psi_{l}(z)f_{l,\varepsilon}^{-(1-\zeta)}(z)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\partial_{t}(f-l)_{+}(z)\eta^{2}(z)f_{l,\varepsilon}^{-(1-\zeta)}(z)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\partial_{t}(f-l)_{-}(z)\xi(v)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t.

We integrate both terms by part. For the first, we get:

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\partial_{t}(f-l)_{+}(z)\eta^{2}(z)f_{l,\varepsilon}^{-(1-\zeta)}(z)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\partial_{t}((f-l)_{+}+\varepsilon)^{\zeta}(z)\eta^{2}(z)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}((f-l)_{+}+\varepsilon)^{\zeta}(z)\partial_{t}\eta^{2}(z)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq CR^{-2s}\int_{Q_{R}}((f-l)_{+}+\varepsilon)^{\zeta}(z)\,{\rm d}z.

For the second term, we find:

(42)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\partial_{t}(f-l)_{-}(z)\xi(v)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big((f-l)_{-}(t_{2})-(f-l)_{-}(t_{1})\big)\xi(v)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big((f-l)(t_{2})-(f-l)(t_{1})\big)\xi(v)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big((f-l)_{+}(t_{2})-(f-l)_{+}(t_{1})\big)\xi(v)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x.

We now want to use the mass conservation and the decay for large velocities. We find

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big((f-l)(t_{2})-(f-l)(t_{1})\big)\xi(v)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big(f(t_{2})-f(t_{1})\big)\xi(v)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big(f(t_{2})-f(t_{1})\big)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big(f(t_{2})-f(t_{1})\big)(\xi-1)(v)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\leq\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}f(t_{1})(1-\xi)(v)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x,

where for last inequality we used the mass conservation and the fact that
\xi\leq 1
.
Then we recall our definition of
\xi
, which implies that
1-\xi=0
in
B_{r}
. Thus

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}f(t_{1})(1-\xi)(v)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{B_{\bar{R}}\setminus B_{r}}f(t_{1})(1-\xi)(v)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x+\int_{B_{R^{1+2s}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{\bar{R}}}f(t_{1})(1-\xi)(v)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\leq C_{q}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{B_{\bar{R}}\setminus B_{r}}\langle v\rangle^{-q}(1-\xi)(v)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x+C_{q}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{\bar{R}}}\langle v\rangle^{-q}\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x.

Since
q>d
,

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{\bar{R}}}\langle v\rangle^{-q}\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\leq CR^{d(1+2s)}{\bar{R}}^{-q+d}.

Moreover, we use that on
B_{\bar{R}}\setminus B_{r}
, the cutoff
(1-\xi)(v)\sim\frac{\left|v\right|-r}{\bar{R}-r}
and
\langle v\rangle\geq\left|v\right|-r
. Thus for
d<q<d+1
,

\displaystyle C_{q}

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{B_{\bar{R}}\setminus B_{r}}\langle v\rangle^{-q}(1-\xi)(v)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{B_{\bar{R}}\setminus B_{r}}\frac{(\left|v\right|-r)^{1-q}}{\bar{R}-r}\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\leq C\omega_{d}R^{d(1+2s)}\frac{1}{\bar{R}-r}\int_{0}^{\bar{R}-r}\rho^{-q+1+d-1}\,{\rm d}\rho

\displaystyle\leq CR^{d(1+2s)}\frac{(\bar{R}-r)^{d-q+1}}{\bar{R}-r}=C\frac{R^{d(1+2s)}}{(\bar{R}-r)^{q-d}}.

We combine these estimates to find:

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big((f-l)(t_{2})-(f-l)(t_{1})\big)\xi(v)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\leq C\frac{R^{d(1+2s)}}{(\bar{R}-r)^{q-d}}+CR^{d(1+2s)}{\bar{R}}^{-q+d}.

This deals with the first part of (
42
). We are left with bounding

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big((f-l)_{+}(t_{2})-(f-l)_{+}(t_{1})\big)\xi(v)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}(f-l)_{+}(t_{1})\xi(v)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x

we use that on
\{f(v)>l\}
, we have
\left|v\right|\leq\left(\frac{C_{q}}{l}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}=\frac{r}{2}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{B_{\frac{r}{2}}}(f-l)_{+}(t_{1})\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x.

We conclude:

\displaystyle\int_{-R^{2s}}^{0}

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\partial_{t}f(z)\psi_{l}(z)f_{l,\varepsilon}^{-(1-\zeta)}(z)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq CR^{-2s}\int_{Q_{R}}((f-l)_{+}+\varepsilon)^{\zeta}(z)\,{\rm d}z+C\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}\frac{R^{d(1+2s)}}{(\bar{R}-r)^{q-d}}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}\int_{B_{2R^{1+2s}}}\int_{B_{r}}(f-l)_{+}(t_{1})\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x.

Overall, we find

(43)

\displaystyle\int_{-R^{2s}}^{0}

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\mathcal{T}f(z)\psi_{l}(z)f_{l,\varepsilon}^{-(1-\zeta)}(z)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq CR^{-2s}\int_{Q_{R}}\big((f-l)_{+}+\varepsilon\big)^{\zeta}(z)\,{\rm d}z+C\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}\frac{R^{d(1+2s)}}{(\bar{R}-r)^{q-d}}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}\int_{B_{2R^{1+2s}}}\int_{B_{r}}(f-l)_{+}(t_{1})\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x.

Step 3: Relation of transport and non-locality.

Finally we use the equation. By (
1
), (
43
), and (
41
) we see

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\int_{-R^{2s}}^{0}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\mathcal{T}f(z)\psi_{l}(z)f_{l,\varepsilon}^{-(1-\zeta)}(z)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle+\int_{-R^{2s}}^{0}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\mathcal{E}\left(f,\psi_{l}f_{l,\varepsilon}^{-(1-\zeta)}\right)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(\zeta)R^{-2s}\int_{Q_{2R}}((f-l)_{+}+\varepsilon)^{\zeta}(z)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle-\int_{-R^{2s}}^{0}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)}\int_{B_{R}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{R}}(f-l)_{+}(w)\eta^{2}(z)\phi(t)f_{l,\varepsilon}^{-(1-\zeta)}(v)K(v,w)\chi_{f(v)>l}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle+C\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}(\bar{R}-r)^{-2s}\int_{Q_{2R}^{v}\times B_{\bar{R}}}f(v)\,{\rm d}z+\frac{\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}}{2}\left(\frac{r}{2}\right)^{-2s}\int_{Q_{2R}^{v}\times B_{\frac{r}{2}}}(f-l)_{+}(v)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle+C\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}\frac{R^{d(1+2s)}}{(\bar{R}-r)^{q-d}}+\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}\int_{B_{2R^{1+2s}}}\int_{B_{r}}(f-l)_{+}(t_{1})\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle+Cl\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}r^{-2s}\left|\{f>l\}\cap Q_{2R}^{v}\times B_{\frac{r}{2}}\right|.

or rearranged, (using that
B_{\frac{R^{1+2s}}{2}}\subset{\mathbb{T}}_{R_{1},R_{2}}(y)
),

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\frac{R}{2}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{R}}(f-l)_{+}(w)f_{l,\varepsilon}^{-(1-\zeta)}(v)K(v,w)\chi_{f(v)>l}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq CR^{-2s}\int_{Q_{2R}}(f-l)_{+}^{\zeta}(z)\,{\rm d}z+CR^{n-2s}\varepsilon^{\zeta}+C\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}\frac{R^{d(1+2s)}}{(\bar{R}-r)^{q-d}}

\displaystyle+C\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}(\bar{R}-r)^{-2s}\int_{Q_{2R}^{v}\times B_{\bar{R}}}f(v)\,{\rm d}z+\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}\left(\frac{r}{2}\right)^{-2s}\int_{Q_{2R}^{v}\times B_{\frac{r}{2}}}(f-l)_{+}(v)\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+Cl\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}r^{-2s}\left|\{f>l\}\cap Q_{2R}^{v}\times B_{\frac{r}{2}}\right|.

We want to pick
\bar{R}
sufficiently large, so that

\frac{1}{(\bar{R}-r)^{q-d}}+\frac{\left\|f\right\|_{L^{1}(Q_{R}^{v}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}{(\bar{R}-r)^{2s}}\leq\frac{2^{1-2s}}{r^{2s}}\int_{Q_{2R}^{v}\times B_{\frac{r}{2}}}(f-l)_{+}(v)\,{\rm d}v.

(The right hand side is strict positive, since with no loss in generality there is at least one point where
f>l
in
Q_{R}\subset Q_{R}^{v}\times B_{\frac{r}{2}}
, so
\bar{R}<\infty
.)
Then, recalling that
2R\leq r
,

(44)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\frac{R}{2}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{R}}(f-l)_{+}(w)f_{l,\varepsilon}^{-(1-\zeta)}(v)K(v,w)\chi_{f(v)>l}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq CR^{-2s}\int_{Q_{2R}}(f-l)_{+}^{\zeta}(z)\,{\rm d}z+CR^{n-2s}\varepsilon^{\zeta}+C\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}r^{-2s}\int_{Q_{2R}^{v}\times B_{\frac{r}{2}}}(f-l)_{+}(v)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle+Cl\varepsilon^{-(1-\zeta)}r^{-2s}\left|\{f>l\}\cap Q_{2R}^{v}\times B_{\frac{r}{2}}\right|.

Now we pick
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small, depending on
l
. Recall
n=2d(1+s)+2s
.

If
l=0
, we pick

\varepsilon\approx r^{-n}\int_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}\,{\rm d}z.

Otherwise, if
l>0
, we pick

(45)

\varepsilon\approx lr^{-n}\left|\big\{f>l\big\}\cap Q_{r}\right|.

In the first case we find

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\frac{R}{2}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{R}}(f-l)_{+}(w)f_{l,\varepsilon}^{-(1-\zeta)}(v)K(v,w)\chi_{f(v)>l}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{-2s}\int_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}^{\zeta}(z)\,{\rm d}z+Cr^{n(1-\zeta)-2s}\left(\int_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}\,{\rm d}z\right)^{\zeta}.

Due to Jensen’s inequality, we find

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}^{\zeta}(z)\,{\rm d}z=r^{n}\left(\frac{1}{r^{n}}\int_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}^{\zeta}(z)\,{\rm d}z\right)^{\frac{1}{\zeta}\zeta}\leq r^{n}\left(\frac{1}{r^{n}}\int_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}(z)\,{\rm d}z\right)^{\zeta},

so that

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\frac{R}{2}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{R}}

\displaystyle(f-l)_{+}(w)f_{l,\varepsilon}^{-(1-\zeta)}(v)K(v,w)\chi_{f(v)>l}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{n(1-\zeta)-2s}\left(\int_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}\,{\rm d}z\right)^{\zeta}.

This implies (
24
) for
l=0
, if we take out the infimum of
(f-l+\varepsilon)^{-(1-\zeta)}
over
z\in Q_{\frac{R}{2}}\cap\chi_{f>l}
on the left hand side, divide by the infimum,
use
(\inf g)^{-1}=\sup g^{-1}
, so that

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\frac{R}{2}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{R}}(f-l)_{+}(w)K(v,w)\chi_{f(v)>l}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{n(1-\zeta)-2s}\left(\sup_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}^{1-\zeta}+r^{-n(1-\zeta)}\left(\int_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}\,{\rm d}z\right)^{1-\zeta}\right)\left(\int_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}\,{\rm d}z\right)^{\zeta}

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{n(1-\zeta)-2s}\sup_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}^{1-\zeta}\left(\int_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}\,{\rm d}z\right)^{\zeta}+Cr^{-2s}\int_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{n(1-\zeta)-2s}\left(\sup_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}\right)^{1-\zeta}\left(\int_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}\,{\rm d}z\right)^{\zeta}.

In the second case, where
l>0
, we find from (
44
) with the choice of (
45
)

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\frac{R}{2}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{R}}(f-l)_{+}(w)f_{l,\varepsilon}^{-(1-\zeta)}(v)K(v,w)\chi_{f(v)>l}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{-2s}\int_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}^{\zeta}(z)\,{\rm d}z+Cl^{\zeta}r^{n(1-\zeta)-2s}\left(1+\frac{\sup_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}}{l}\right)\left|\big\{f>l\big\}\cap Q_{r}\right|^{\zeta}.

As before, we take out the infimum of
(f-l+\varepsilon)^{-(1-\zeta)}
over
z\in Q_{\frac{R}{2}}\cap\chi_{f>l}
on the left hand side, and find

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\frac{R}{2}}}

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{R}}(f-l)_{+}(w)\chi_{f>l}(v)K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{-2s}\left(\sup_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}\right)^{1-\zeta}\int_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}^{\zeta}(z)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle+C\left(\sup_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}\right)^{1-\zeta}l^{\zeta}r^{n(1-\zeta)-2s}\left(1+\frac{\sup_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}}{l}\right)\left|\big\{f>l\big\}\cap Q_{r}\right|^{\zeta}

\displaystyle+Cl^{1-\zeta}r^{-n(1-\zeta)-2s}\left|\big\{f>l\big\}\cap Q_{r}\right|^{1-\zeta}\int_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}^{\zeta}(z)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle+Clr^{-2s}\left(1+\frac{\sup_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}}{l}\right)\left|\big\{f>l\big\}\cap Q_{r}\right|.

Again, due to Jensen’s inequality

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}^{\zeta}(z)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq r^{n}\left(\frac{1}{r^{n}}\int_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}(z)\,{\rm d}z\right)^{\zeta}

\displaystyle\leq r^{n(1-\zeta)}\left(\sup_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}\right)^{\zeta}\left|\{f>l\}\cap Q_{r}\right|^{\zeta},

and

\int_{Q_{f}}(f-l)_{+}^{\zeta}(z)\,{\rm d}z\leq\sup_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}^{\zeta}\left|\big\{f>l\big\}\cap Q_{r}\right|\leq\left(\sup_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}\right)^{\zeta}\left|\big\{f>l\big\}\cap Q_{r}\right|,

so that

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\frac{R}{2}}}

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{R}}(f-l)_{+}(w)\chi_{f>l}(v)K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{\sup_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}}{l}\right)^{1-\zeta}r^{n(1-\zeta)-2s}\left(l+\sup_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}\right)\left|\big\{f>l\big\}\cap Q_{r}\right|^{\zeta}

\displaystyle+Cr^{-2s}\left(l+\sup_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}+l\left(\frac{\sup_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}}{l}\right)^{\zeta}\right)\left|\big\{f>l\big\}\cap Q_{r}\right|

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{n(1-\zeta)-2s}\left[\left(\frac{\sup_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}}{l}\right)^{1-\zeta}+\left(\frac{\sup_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}}{l}\right)^{\zeta}+1\right]

\displaystyle\times\left(l+\sup_{Q_{r}}(f-l)_{+}\right)\left|\big\{f>l\big\}\cap Q_{r}\right|^{\zeta}.

This concludes the proof of (
25
), and therefore of the proposition.

∎

4.
De Giorgi in
L^{1}

We aim to derive De Giorgi’s first lemma, which is typically a bound from
L^{2}
to
L^{\infty}
. Such a boundedness result has been derived in
[
35
, Lemma 4.1]
before, however, with a tail quantity on the right hand side. We aim to derive a fully local boundedness result. Since we saw in the previous section how to bound the tail in
L^{1}
, we perform the De Giorgi iteration in
L^{1}
. The result is Proposition
5
.

The De Giorgi argument is based on the realisation that for any sub-solution
f
of (
1
), the level set function
(f-l)_{+}
for any
l\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
is still a sub-solution to (
1
). Then the argument relies on three steps.
The first is the energy estimate, which is typically derived by testing the equation with the solution itself, and thus yields a bound in
L^{2}
. This is required to estimate the coercive term, which for our equation is the
L^{2}_{t,x}H^{s}_{v}
norm of the solution. But even then, by Hölder’s inequality, the tail term naturally appears in
L^{1}
, if we pay the supremum on the test function. Thus, we can use the non-local to local bound of Proposition
11
, to obtain a fully local energy estimate. The second step is the gain of integrability. This is usually done on the same Lebesgue level as the energy estimate, that is
L^{2}
. Since this only works for solutions on the whole space, we need to localise with a cutoff. This, however, naturally makes appear another tail term, which then, would need to be bounded in
L^{2}
, which we don’t know how to bound. Therefore, we need a gain of integrability in
L^{1}
. Then, on the one hand, the tail terms are again bounded by Proposition
11
. On the other hand, we then also have to bound the
L^{1}_{t,x}W^{s,1}_{v}
norm of the localised level set function, which can be done using Cauchy-Schwarz and the energy estimate. The last step is the proper choice of control quantity, which for us is the
L^{1}
norm of the level set functions, which by Chebyshev’s inequality, can be related to the measure of the upper level set.

4.1.
Gain of integrability in
L^{1}

The preliminary step we require for the iteration is thus a gain of integrability in
L^{1}
. For this, we consider the fractional Kolmogorov equation (
8
), which plays the role of the constant coefficient equation for (
1
), with a right hand side
h=h_{1}+h_{2}
for some
h_{2}\in L^{1}\big([0,\tau]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}\big)
and some
h_{1}
such that

\left\|(-\Delta_{v})^{-\frac{s+\epsilon}{2}}h_{1}\right\|_{L^{1}([0,\tau]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}<+\infty

for
0\leq\epsilon<s
. The role of the
\epsilon
is the number of derivatives that we can put on the fundamental solution so that these are absorbed in the time integrability.
We prove:

Proposition 13
.

Let
0\leq f
solve (or be a sub-solution of) (
8
) in
[0,\tau]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
, with
f(0,x,v)=f_{0}(x,v)\in L^{1}\cap L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
, and with
h=h_{1}+h_{2}
where
h_{1},h_{2}\in L^{1}\cap L^{2}([-\tau,0]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
such that

\left\|(-\Delta_{v})^{-\frac{s+\epsilon}{2}}h_{1}\right\|_{L^{1}([-\tau,0]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}<+\infty.

Then for any
0\leq\epsilon<s
and any
1\leq p<1+\frac{s-\epsilon}{s+2d(s+1)+\epsilon}
there holds

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}([0,\tau]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\tau^{\frac{1}{p}-\alpha_{\epsilon}}\left\|(-\Delta_{v})^{-\frac{s+\epsilon}{2}}h_{1}\right\|_{L^{1}([-\tau,0]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}+\tau^{\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{2}-\alpha_{0}}\left\|h_{2}\right\|_{L^{1}([0,\tau]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}

\displaystyle+\tau^{\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{2}-\alpha_{0}}\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})},

where
\alpha_{\epsilon}=d\left(1+\frac{1}{s}\right)\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)+\frac{s+\epsilon}{2s}
.

Proof.

Equation (
8
) admits a fundamental solution, given by

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq f(t,x,v)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d+1}}\big[h_{1}(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},v^{\prime})+h_{2}(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},v^{\prime})\big]J\left(t-t^{\prime},x-x^{\prime}-(t-t^{\prime})v^{\prime},v-v^{\prime}\right)\,{\rm d}t^{\prime}\,{\rm d}x^{\prime}\,{\rm d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f_{0}(x^{\prime},v^{\prime})J\left(t,x-x^{\prime}-tv^{\prime},v-v^{\prime}\right)\,{\rm d}x^{\prime}\,{\rm d}v^{\prime},

where
(t,x,v)\in(0,\tau)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
and

(46)

J(t,x,v)=\frac{C_{d}}{t^{d+\frac{d}{s}}}\mathcal{J}\left(\frac{x}{t^{1+\frac{1}{2s}}},\frac{v}{t^{\frac{1}{2s}}}\right),\qquad\hat{\mathcal{J}}(\varphi,\xi)=\exp\left(-\int_{0}^{1}\left|\xi-\tau\varphi\right|^{2s}\,{\rm d}\tau\right).

We remark that for any
r\geq 1
there holds for
t>0
and for any
\epsilon\geq 0

(47)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\left\|J(t,\cdot,\cdot)\right\|_{L^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}=t^{-d(1+\frac{1}{s})(1-\frac{1}{r})}\left\|\mathcal{J}\right\|_{L^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})},

\displaystyle\left\|(-\Delta)^{\frac{s+\epsilon}{2}}_{v}J(t,\cdot,\cdot)\right\|_{L^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}=t^{-d(1+\frac{1}{s})(1-\frac{1}{r})-\frac{s+\epsilon}{2s}}\left\|(-\Delta)^{\frac{s+\epsilon}{2}}_{v}\mathcal{J}\right\|_{L^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}.

(The factor
t^{-d(1+\frac{1}{s})}
comes from the prefactor in (
46
). The fractional derivative
(-\Delta)^{\frac{s+\epsilon}{2}}_{v}
gives a factor in time of
t^{-\frac{s+\epsilon}{2s}}
. Finally the
L^{r}
norm in space and velocity scales as
t^{\frac{d}{r}(\frac{2+2s}{2s})}
.)

We define the modified convolution

\displaystyle f*_{t}g(x,v):=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f(x^{\prime},v^{\prime})g(x-x^{\prime}-tv^{\prime},v-v^{\prime})\,{\rm d}x^{\prime}\,{\rm d}v^{\prime}.

Note that the modified convolution satisfies the usual Young inequality independent of
t
:

\displaystyle\left\|f*_{t}g\right\|_{L^{r}_{x,v}}\leq\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{q}_{x,v}}

for
1+\frac{1}{r}=\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}
. We split
f=\tilde{f}_{0}+f_{1}+f_{2}
with

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{0}(t,\cdot,\cdot):=f_{0}*_{t}J(t,\cdot,\cdot),

\displaystyle f_{1}(t,\cdot,\cdot):=\int_{0}^{t}(-\Delta)_{v}^{-\frac{s+\epsilon}{2}}h_{1}*_{(t-t^{\prime})}(-\Delta)_{v}^{\frac{s+\epsilon}{2}}J(t-t^{\prime},\cdot,\cdot)\,{\rm d}t^{\prime},

\displaystyle f_{2}(t,\cdot,\cdot):=\int_{0}^{t}h_{2}*_{(t-t^{\prime})}J(t-t^{\prime},\cdot,\cdot)\,{\rm d}t^{\prime}.

By Young’s inequality we get for
\alpha_{0}=d\left(1+\frac{1}{s}\right)\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)+\frac{1}{2}
and for
\alpha_{\epsilon}=d\left(1+\frac{1}{s}\right)\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)+\frac{s+\epsilon}{2s}

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{f}_{0}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\right\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}\left\|J(t,\cdot,\cdot)\right\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}=\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}\left\|\mathcal{J}\right\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}t^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha_{0}},

\displaystyle\left\|f_{1}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\right\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left\|(-\Delta)_{v}^{-\frac{s+\epsilon}{2}}h_{1}(t^{\prime})\right\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}\left\|(-\Delta)_{v}^{\frac{s+\epsilon}{2}}J(t-t^{\prime},\cdot,\cdot)\right\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}\,{\rm d}t^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\left\|(-\Delta)_{v}^{-\frac{s+\epsilon}{2}}h_{1}(t^{\prime})\right\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}\left\|(-\Delta)_{v}^{\frac{s+\epsilon}{2}}\mathcal{J}\right\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}(t-t^{\prime})^{-\alpha_{\epsilon}}\,{\rm d}t^{\prime},

\displaystyle\left\|f_{2}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\right\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left\|h_{2}(t^{\prime})\right\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}\left\|J(t-t^{\prime},\cdot,\cdot)\right\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}\,{\rm d}t^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\left\|h_{2}(t^{\prime})\right\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}\left\|\mathcal{J}\right\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}(t-t^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha_{0}}\,{\rm d}t^{\prime}.

Since
p\left(\frac{1}{2}-\alpha_{0}\right)>-1
we get that
\tilde{f}_{0}\in L^{p}([0,\tau]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
and

\left\|\tilde{f}_{0}\right\|_{L^{p}([0,\tau]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}\leq C\tau^{\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{2}-\alpha_{0}}\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}.

For
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
we apply Young’s inequality again and get

\displaystyle\left\|f_{1}\right\|_{L^{p}([0,\tau]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}\leq C\left\|(-\Delta)_{v}^{-\frac{s+\epsilon}{2}}h_{1}\right\|_{L^{1}([0,\tau]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}\tau^{-\alpha_{\epsilon}+\frac{1}{p}},

\displaystyle\left\|f_{2}\right\|_{L^{p}([0,\tau]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}\leq C\left\|h_{2}\right\|_{L^{1}([0,\tau]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}\tau^{\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{2}-\alpha_{0}},

provided that
\frac{1}{p}-\alpha_{\epsilon}>0
. Equivalently, we require

1\leq p<1+\frac{s-\epsilon}{s+2d(s+1)+\epsilon}.

∎

4.2.
De Giorgi iteration

This section is dedicated to the proof of Proposition
5
.
We use the tail bound from Proposition
11
to get a local energy estimate. Then we use the gain of integrability in
L^{1}
from Proposition
13
, by comparing the solution of (
1
) to the fractional Kolmogorov equation (
8
). We bound the right hand side by the local energy estimate, and the tail term again with Proposition
11
. Finally, we iterate the so gained local a priori estimate on level set functions.

In this section, we let
f
solve (
1
) in
[-R^{2s}+t_{0},t_{0}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}(x_{0}+(t-t_{0})v_{0})\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
such that
f
conserves mass.
With no loss in generality, we set
z_{0}=(0,0,0)
. We, moreover, work with zero source term. Note that the energy estimate, the gain of integrability, and correspondingly the De Giorgi iteration, have to be modified in a suitable way to account for non-zero source.

Step 1: Local energy estimate.

Let
\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d})
be such that
\phi(t,x,v)=0
for
(t,x,v)
outside
Q_{\frac{R}{4}}
and
\phi(t,x,v)=1
for
(t,x,v)\in Q_{\frac{R}{8}}
. We then test (
1
) with
(f-l)_{+}\phi^{2}
, so that

(48)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}\mathcal{T}f(z)\phi^{2}(z)(f-l)_{+}(z)\,{\rm d}z-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}\mathcal{L}f(z)(f-l)_{+}(z)\phi^{2}(z)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}\mathcal{T}(f-l)^{2}_{+}(z)\phi^{2}(z)\,{\rm d}z-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}\mathcal{L}f(z)(f-l)_{+}(z)\phi^{2}(z)\,{\rm d}z.

We observe that for any
\frac{R}{4}<r
, if we abbreviate
(f-l)_{+}=:{f_{l}}_{+}
and if we denote by
\Omega_{\rho}:=[-\rho^{2s},0]\times B_{\rho^{1+2s}}
, then due to (
17
)

(49)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}\mathcal{L}f(z)(f-l)_{+}(z)\phi^{2}(z)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big[f(t,x,v)-f(t,x,w)\big]\big((f-l)_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(t,x,v)K(t,x,v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}}\int_{B_{r}}\int_{B_{r}}\big[f(v)-f(w)\big]\Big[\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(v)-\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(w)\Big]K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}}\int_{B_{r}}\int_{B_{r}}\big[f(v)-f(w)\big]\Big[\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(v)+\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(w)\Big]K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}}\int_{B_{r}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{r}}\big[f(v)-f(w)\big]\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(z)K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}}\int_{B_{r}}\int_{B_{r}}\big[f(v)-f(w)\big]\Big[\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(v)-\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(w)\Big]

\displaystyle\times\big[K(v,w)+K(w,v)\big]\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}}\int_{B_{r}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{r}}\big[f(v)-f(w)\big]\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(z)K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle=:\mathcal{E}_{loc}+\mathcal{E}_{tail}.

Step 1-i: Transport.

We integrate by parts the transport term, and use
\left|\mathcal{T}\phi\right|\sim R^{-2s}
.

(50)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}\mathcal{T}(f-l)^{2}_{+}(z)\phi^{2}(z)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}(f-l)^{2}_{+}(z)\mathcal{T}\phi^{2}(z)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq CR^{-2s}\int_{Q_{\frac{R}{4}}}(f-l)^{2}_{+}(z)\,{\rm d}z.

Step 1-ii: Tail bound.

We use (
25
) from Proposition
11
for
f
, which we assume to be a solution, in particular a super-solution, so that for
r=\frac{R}{2}

(51)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{tail}=\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}}\int_{B_{r}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{r}}\big[f(w)-f(v)\big]\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(z)K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq\int_{Q_{\frac{R}{4}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{\frac{R}{2}}}\big[f(t,x,w)-l\big]\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(z)K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{Q_{\frac{R}{4}}}(f-l)_{+}\int_{Q_{\frac{R}{4}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{\frac{R}{2}}}(f-l)_{+}(t,x,w)K(v,w)\chi_{f>l}(v)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq CR^{n(1-\zeta)-2s}\sup_{Q_{\frac{R}{4}}}(f-l)_{+}\mathcal{M}_{l}\left|\big\{f>l\big\}\cap Q_{R}\right|^{\zeta},

where we recall
\mathcal{M}_{l}
is given in (
26
).

Step 1-iii: Not-too-non-local operator.

What remains to be estimated is the local contribution of the non-local operator,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{loc}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{B_{r}}\int_{B_{r}}\big[f(v)-f(w)\big]\Big[\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(v)-\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(w)\Big]K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z,

where we recall
{f_{l}}_{+}=(f-l)_{+}
.

Claim.
For
z=(t,x,v)
and
r=\frac{R}{2}

(52)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{loc}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{B_{r}}\int_{B_{r}}\Big[\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi\big)(v)-\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi\big)(w)\Big]^{2}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}v-Cr^{-2s}\int_{B_{r}}{f_{l}}_{+}^{2}(v)\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{B_{r}}\int_{B_{r}}\big[{f_{l}}_{-}(v)-{f_{l}}_{-}(w)\big]\Big[\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(v)-\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(w)\Big]K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v.

Indeed, since

(53)

{f_{l}}_{-}(v)-{f_{l}}_{-}(w)=f(v)-f(w)-\big({f_{l}}_{+}(v)-{f_{l}}_{+}(w)\big),

and

\displaystyle\Big[\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi\big)(v)-\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi\big)(w)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle-\big[{f_{l}}_{+}(v)-{f_{l}}_{+}(w)\big]\Big[\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(v)-\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(w)\Big]

\displaystyle={f_{l}}_{+}(v){f_{l}}_{+}(w)\big[\phi(v)-\phi(w)\big]^{2},

there holds

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}}

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}}\Bigg\{\Big[\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi\big)(v)-\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi\big)(w)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle+\big[{f_{l}}_{-}(v)-{f_{l}}_{-}(w)\big]\Big[\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(v)-\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(w)\Big]\Bigg\}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{r}}\int_{B_{r}}\Bigg\{\Big[\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi\big)(v)-\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi\big)(w)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle-\big[{f_{l}}_{+}(v)-{f_{l}}_{+}(w)\big]\Big[\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(v)-\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(w)\Big]\Bigg\}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{r}}\int_{B_{r}}\big[f(v)-f(w)\big]\Big[\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(v)-\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(w)\Big]K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{r}}\int_{B_{r}}{f_{l}}_{+}(v){f_{l}}_{+}(w)\big[\phi(v)-\phi(w)\big]^{2}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v+2\mathcal{E}_{loc}.

Thus, using Young’s inequality and (
16
), we further bound

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}}

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}}\int_{B_{r}}{f_{l}}_{+}(v){f_{l}}_{+}(w)\big[\phi(v)-\phi(w)\big]^{2}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\phi\right\|_{C^{1}_{v}}^{2}\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}}\int_{B_{r}}\int_{B_{r}}\Big({f_{l}}^{2}_{+}(v)+{f_{l}}^{2}_{+}(w)\Big)\left|v-w\right|^{2}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{-2}r^{2-2s}\int_{Q_{\frac{R}{2}}}{f_{l}}_{+}^{2}(z)\,{\rm d}z.

This proves (
52
).

In particular, (
52
) implies for
r=\frac{R}{2}

(54)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{loc}=\iint_{B_{r}\times B_{r}}\Big[f(v)-f(w)\Big]\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(v)K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\iint_{B_{r}\times B_{r}}\Big[\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi\big)(v)-\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi\big)(w)\Big]^{2}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v-Cr^{-2s}\int_{B_{r}}{f_{l}}_{+}^{2}(v)\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\iint_{B_{r}\times B_{r}}\big[{f_{l}}_{-}(v)-{f_{l}}_{-}(w)\big]\Big[\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(v)-\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(w)\Big]K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\iint_{B_{r}\times B_{r}}\Big[\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi\big)(v)-\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi\big)(w)\Big]^{2}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v-Cr^{-2s}\int_{B_{r}}{f_{l}}_{+}^{2}(v)\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle-\iint_{B_{r}\times B_{r}}\Big[{f_{l}}_{-}(v)\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(w)+{f_{l}}_{-}(w)\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(v)\Big]K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\iint_{B_{r}\times B_{r}}\Big[\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi\big)(v)-\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi\big)(w)\Big]^{2}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v-Cr^{-2s}\int_{B_{r}}{f_{l}}_{+}^{2}(v)\,{\rm d}v.

In the last inequality we used for each
v,w\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}

-{f_{l}}_{-}(w)\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(v)-{f_{l}}_{-}(v)\big({f_{l}}_{+}\phi^{2}\big)(w)\geq 0.

Step 1-iv: Conclusion.

Combining (
48
) and (
49
) with (
50
)
of
Step 1-i
, (
51
) of
Step 1-ii
, and (
54
) of
Step 1-iii
, we conclude

(55)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}}\iint_{B_{\frac{R}{2}}\times B_{\frac{R}{2}}}\Big[\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(v)-\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(w)\Big]^{2}K(t,x,v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq CR^{-2s}\int_{Q_{\frac{R}{4}}}(f-l)_{+}^{2}(z)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle+CR^{n(1-\zeta)-2s}\sup_{Q_{\frac{R}{4}}}(f-l)_{+}\mathcal{M}_{l}\left|\big\{f>l\big\}\cap Q_{R}\right|^{\zeta}.

Step 2: Gain of Integrability.

For the gain of integrability, we use Proposition
13
.
We check that
(f-l)_{+}\phi
satisfies in
{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\big[(f-l)_{+}\phi\big]

\displaystyle+(-\Delta_{v})^{s}\big[(f-l)_{+}\phi\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{L}\big[(f-l)_{+}\phi\big]+(-\Delta_{v})^{s}\big[(f-l)_{+}\phi\big]+(f-l)_{+}\mathcal{T}\phi

\displaystyle+\Bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{\rho}(v)}\big(f(w)-l\big)_{+}K(v,w)\big(\phi(v)-\phi(w)\big)\,{\rm d}w\Bigg)\chi_{f>l}\tilde{\eta}(v)

\displaystyle+\Bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big(f(w)-l\big)_{+}K(v,w)\big(\phi(v)-\phi(w)\big)\,{\rm d}w\Bigg)\chi_{f>l}\big(1-\tilde{\eta}(v)\big)

\displaystyle+\Bigg(\int_{B_{\rho}(v)}\big(f(w)-l\big)_{+}K(v,w)\big(\phi(v)-\phi(w)\big)\,{\rm d}w\Bigg)\chi_{f>l}\tilde{\eta}(v),

where
0<\rho\leq R/8
,
\tilde{\eta}=1
in
B_{\frac{R}{4}+2\rho}
and
\tilde{\eta}=0
outside
B_{\frac{R}{4}+3\rho}
.

|v|
1-\tilde{\eta}
\tilde{\eta}
