Figure 3.
The cutoffs
\eta,\tilde{\eta}\in C_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
in velocity that are used in the proof of Proposition
5
.

The only non-local term in the inequality above is on the third line. Note that
\phi(v)-\phi(w)\leq 0
for
v
outside
B_{\frac{R}{4}}
and
w
outside
B_{\rho}(v)
, so that the tail term arising from the equation (on the third line) is non-negative only for
v\in B_{\frac{R}{4}}
, where
\tilde{\eta}(v)=1
.
Furthermore, we note since
\phi
and
1-\tilde{\eta}
have disjoint support, there holds
\phi(v)(1-\tilde{\eta}(v))=0
for all
v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
. Thus, since
0\leq 1-\tilde{\eta}\leq 1
, we see that

\Bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big(f(w)-l\big)_{+}K(v,w)\big(\phi(v)-\phi(w)\big)\,{\rm d}w\Bigg)\chi_{f>l}\big(1-\tilde{\eta}(v)\big)\leq 0.

Therefore, there holds for
(t,x,v)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}

\displaystyle\big[(f-l)_{+}\phi\big]+(-\Delta_{v})^{s}\big[(f-l)_{+}\phi\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{L}\big[(f-l)_{+}\phi\big]+(-\Delta_{v})^{s}\big[(f-l)_{+}\phi\big]+(f-l)_{+}\mathcal{T}\phi

\displaystyle+\Bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{\rho}(v)}\big(f(w)-l\big)_{+}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\Bigg)\chi_{f>l}\phi

\displaystyle+\Bigg(\int_{B_{\rho}(v)}\big(f(w)-l\big)_{+}K(v,w)\big(\phi(v)-\phi(w)\big)\,{\rm d}w\Bigg)\chi_{f>l}\tilde{\eta}(v)

\displaystyle=:h_{1}+h_{2},

with

\displaystyle h_{1}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{L}\big[(f-l)_{+}\phi\big]+(-\Delta_{v})^{s}\big[(f-l)_{+}\phi\big],

\displaystyle h_{2}

\displaystyle:=(f-l)_{+}\mathcal{T}\phi+\Bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{\rho}(v)}\big(f(w)-l\big)_{+}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\Bigg)\chi_{f>l}\phi

\displaystyle+\Bigg(\int_{B_{\rho}(v)}\big(f(w)-l\big)_{+}K(v,w)\big(\phi(v)-\phi(w)\big)\,{\rm d}w\Bigg)\chi_{f>l}\tilde{\eta}(v).

Proposition
13
then implies for any
0<\epsilon<s
and any
1<p<1+\frac{s-\epsilon}{2d(1+s)+s+\epsilon}

(56)

\displaystyle\left\|(f-l)_{+}\phi\right\|_{L^{p}([0,\tau]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}\leq

\displaystyle\tau^{\frac{1}{p}-\alpha_{\epsilon}}\left\|(-\Delta_{v})^{-\frac{s+\epsilon}{2}}h_{1}\right\|_{L^{1}([0,\tau]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}

\displaystyle+\tau^{\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{2}-\alpha_{0}}\left\|h_{2}\right\|_{L^{1}([0,\tau]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}

\displaystyle+\tau^{\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{2}-\alpha_{0}}\left\|\big[(f-l)_{+}\phi\big](0)\right\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})},

where
\alpha_{\epsilon}=d\left(1+\frac{1}{s}\right)\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)+\frac{s+\epsilon}{2s}
and, in our case,
\tau\sim R^{2s}
.

Step 2-i: Contribution of
h_{1}
.

To bound
h_{1}
, we have to estimate, on the one hand,

\displaystyle\left\|(-\Delta_{v})^{-\frac{s+\epsilon}{2}}(-\Delta_{v})^{s}\big[(f-l)_{+}\phi\big]\right\|_{L^{1}([0,\tau]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}

\displaystyle=\left\|(-\Delta_{v})^{\frac{s-\epsilon}{2}}\big[(f-l)_{+}\phi\big]\right\|_{L^{1}([0,\tau]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}}\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\frac{\left|(f-l)_{+}\phi(v)-(f-l)_{+}\phi(w)\right|}{\left|v-w\right|^{d+s-\epsilon}}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z.

On the other hand,
we note that
(-\Delta_{v})^{-\frac{s+\epsilon}{2}}\mathcal{L}
is a non-local operator with a kernel
K(v,w)\left|v-w\right|^{s+\epsilon}
, which satisfies the same ellipticity assumptions as
K
with an improved order
s-\epsilon
(instead of
2s
). In particular, to bound
h_{1}
it suffices to bound for
r=\frac{R}{2}

(57)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\left\|(-\Delta_{v})^{-\frac{s+\epsilon}{2}}\mathcal{L}\big[(f-l)_{+}\phi\big]\right\|_{L^{1}([0,\tau]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}}(-\Delta_{v})^{-\frac{s+\epsilon}{2}}\mathcal{L}\big[(f-l)_{+}\phi\big](z)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\left|\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(w)-\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(v)\right|K(v,w)\left|v-w\right|^{s+\epsilon}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}\times B_{r}}\int_{B_{r}}\dots+\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{r}}\int_{B_{r}}\dots+\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}\times B_{r}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{r}}\dots.

Then, we further note, since
\textrm{supp }\phi\subseteq Q_{\frac{R}{4}}
and
r=\frac{R}{2}
, by (
16
)

(58)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{r}}\int_{B_{r}}\left|\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(w)-\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(v)\right|K(v,w)\left|v-w\right|^{s+\epsilon}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}\times B_{r}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{r}}\left|\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(w)-\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(v)\right|K(v,w)\left|v-w\right|^{s+\epsilon}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{\frac{R}{2}}}\int_{B_{\frac{R}{4}}}\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(w)K(v,w)\left|v-w\right|^{s+\epsilon}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle+\int_{Q_{\frac{R}{4}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{\frac{R}{2}}}\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(v)K(v,w)\left|v-w\right|^{s+\epsilon}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq CR^{-s+\epsilon}\int_{Q_{\frac{R}{4}}}(f-l)_{+}\,{\rm d}z.

Moreover, we introduce
\mathcal{C}_{K}
to be such that

(59)

K^{2}(v,w)\mathcal{C}_{K}^{-1}(v,w)\left|v-w\right|^{2s+d}\sim K(v,w).

For the Boltzmann equation for instance (see
[
24
]
), we would have

\mathcal{C}_{K}(v,w)=\int_{w^{\prime}\perp w-v}f(v+w^{\prime})\left|w^{\prime}\right|^{\gamma+2s+1}\tilde{b}(\cos\theta)\,{\rm d}w^{\prime}.

In particular, due to the ellipticity of
K
in (
16
), we know that

\int_{B_{r}(v)}\mathcal{C}_{K}(v,w)\left|v-w\right|^{-d+2\epsilon}\,{\rm d}w\leq C(\Lambda_{0},\epsilon)r^{2\epsilon}.

Thus

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}\times B_{r}}\int_{B_{r}}\left|\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(w)-\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(v)\right|K(v,w)\left|v-w\right|^{s+\epsilon}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}\times B_{r}}\int_{B_{r}}\left(\chi_{f>l}(t,x,v)+\chi_{f>l}(t,x,w)\right)

\displaystyle\times\left|\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(w)-\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(v)\right|K(v,w)\left|v-w\right|^{s+\epsilon}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}\times B_{r}}\chi_{f>l}(t,x,v)\left(\int_{B_{r}}\left|\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(w)-\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(v)\right|^{2}K^{2}(v,w)\mathcal{C}_{K}^{-1}\left|v-w\right|^{2s+d}\,{\rm d}w\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\int_{B_{r}}\left|v-w\right|^{-d+2\epsilon}\mathcal{C}_{K}\,{\rm d}w\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}\times B_{r}}\chi_{f>l}(t,x,w)\left(\int_{B_{r}}\left|\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(w)-\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(v)\right|^{2}K^{2}(v,w)\mathcal{C}_{K}^{-1}\left|v-w\right|^{2s+d}\,{\rm d}v\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\int_{B_{r}}\left|v-w\right|^{-d+2\epsilon}\mathcal{C}_{K}\,{\rm d}v\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq 2C(\Lambda_{0},\epsilon)r^{\epsilon}\left|\{f>l\}\cap Q_{\frac{R}{2}}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}\times B_{r}}\int_{B_{r}}\left|\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(w)-\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(v)\right|^{2}K^{2}(v,w)C_{K}^{-1}\left|v-w\right|^{2s+d}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

By choice of
\mathcal{C}_{K}
in (
59
), we then relate the last integral to the
H^{s}_{v}
norm of
(f-l)_{+}\phi
, which, due to the energy estimate (
55
), is bounded as

(60)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}\times B_{r}}\int_{B_{r}}\left|\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(w)-\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(v)\right|K(v,w)\left|v-w\right|^{s}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq CR^{\epsilon}\left|\{f>l\}\cap Q_{\frac{R}{2}}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}\times B_{r}}\int_{B_{r}}\left|\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(w)-\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(v)\right|^{2}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq CR^{-s+\epsilon}\left|\{f>l\}\cap Q_{\frac{R}{2}}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|(f-l)_{+}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}(Q_{\frac{3R}{4}})}

\displaystyle+CR^{\frac{n(1-\zeta)}{2}-s+\epsilon}\left(\sup_{Q_{\frac{R}{4}}}(f-l)_{+}\mathcal{M}_{l}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\big\{f>l\big\}\cap Q_{R}\right|^{\frac{\zeta+1}{2}}.

Thus we combine (
57
), (
58
) and (
60
) to get

(61)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\left\|(-\Delta_{v})^{-\frac{s+\epsilon}{2}}\mathcal{L}\big[(f-l)_{+}\phi\big]\right\|_{L^{1}([0,\tau]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\left|\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(w)-\big((f-l)_{+}\phi\big)(v)\right|K(v,w)\left|v-w\right|^{s}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq CR^{-s+\epsilon}\int_{Q_{\frac{R}{4}}}(f-l)_{+}\,{\rm d}z+CR^{-s+\epsilon}\left|\{f>l\}\cap Q_{\frac{R}{2}}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|(f-l)_{+}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}(Q_{\frac{3R}{4}})}

\displaystyle+CR^{\frac{n(1-\zeta)}{2}-s+\epsilon}\left(\sup_{Q_{\frac{R}{4}}}(f-l)_{+}\mathcal{M}_{l}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\big\{f>l\big\}\cap Q_{R}\right|^{\frac{\zeta+1}{2}}.

We conclude thus the bound for
h_{1}
using (
61
):

(62)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\left\|(-\Delta_{v})^{-\frac{s+\epsilon}{2}}h_{1}\right\|_{L^{1}([0,\tau]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}

\displaystyle\leq CR^{-s+\epsilon}\left\|(f-l)_{+}\right\|_{L^{1}(Q_{\frac{R}{4}})}+CR^{-s+\epsilon}\left|\{f>l\}\cap Q_{\frac{R}{2}}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|(f-l)_{+}\phi\right\|_{L^{2}(Q_{\frac{3R}{4}})}

\displaystyle+CR^{\frac{n(1-\zeta)}{2}-s+\epsilon}\left(\sup_{Q_{\frac{R}{4}}}(f-l)_{+}\mathcal{M}_{l}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\big\{f>l\big\}\cap Q_{R}\right|^{\frac{\zeta+1}{2}}.

Step 2-ii: Contribution of
h_{2}
.

To bound
h_{2}
, we distinguish the transport term, and the tail, which is non-singular, from the local contribution, which is singular,

\displaystyle h_{2}

\displaystyle:=\underbrace{(f-l)_{+}\mathcal{T}\phi}_{=:h_{2}^{\rm{transport}}}+\underbrace{\Bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{\rho}(v)}\big(f(w)-l\big)_{+}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\Bigg)\chi_{f>l}\phi}_{=:h_{2}^{\textrm{tail}}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\Bigg(\int_{B_{\rho}(v)}\big(f(w)-l\big)_{+}K(v,w)\big(\phi(v)-\phi(w)\big)\,{\rm d}w\Bigg)\chi_{f>l}\tilde{\eta}(v)}_{=:h_{2}^{\textrm{loc}}},

The transport term gives

(63)

\displaystyle\left\|h_{2}^{\rm{transport}}\right\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d})}\leq CR^{-2s}\left\|(f-l)_{+}\right\|_{L^{1}(Q_{\frac{R}{2}})}.

Then we bound
h_{2}^{\rm{loc}}
using the commutator estimates of Lemma
8
:
we find for
\rho=\frac{R}{16}

\displaystyle\left\|h_{2}^{\rm{loc}}\right\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d})}

\displaystyle=\int_{Q_{\frac{R}{2}}}\Bigg(\int_{B_{\rho}(v)}\big(f(w)-l\big)_{+}K(v,w)\big(\phi(v)-\phi(w)\big)\,{\rm d}w\Bigg)\chi_{f>l}\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq\left|\{f>l\}\cap Q_{\frac{R}{2}}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{Q_{\frac{R}{2}}}\Bigg(\int_{B_{\rho}(v)}\big(f(w)-l\big)_{+}K(v,w)\big(\phi(v)-\phi(w)\big)\,{\rm d}w\Bigg)^{2}\,{\rm d}z\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq CR^{-2s}\left|\{f>l\}\cap Q_{\frac{R}{2}}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|(f-l)_{+}\right\|_{L^{2}(Q_{\frac{R}{2}})}

\displaystyle+CR^{-s}\left|\{f>l\}\cap Q_{\frac{R}{2}}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|(f-l)_{+}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}H^{s}_{v}(Q_{\frac{R}{2}})},

which again by the coercivity (
15
) and the energy estimate (
55
) is bounded as

(64)

\displaystyle\left\|h_{2}^{\rm{loc}}\right\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d})}

\displaystyle\leq CR^{-2s}\left|\{f>l\}\cap Q_{\frac{R}{2}}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|(f-l)_{+}\right\|_{L^{2}(Q_{\frac{R}{2}})}

\displaystyle+CR^{\frac{n(1-\zeta)}{2}-s}\left(\sup_{Q_{\frac{R}{4}}}(f-l)_{+}\mathcal{M}_{l}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\big\{f>l\big\}\cap Q_{R}\right|^{\frac{\zeta+1}{2}}.

Finally, to bound the contribution of the tail term, we use the bound (
25
) from Proposition
11
on
f
:

(65)

\displaystyle\left\|h_{2}^{\rm{tail}}\right\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d})}

\displaystyle=\int_{Q_{\frac{R}{4}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{\rho}(v)}\big(f(w)-l\big)_{+}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\chi_{f>l}\phi\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq CR^{n(1-\zeta)-2s}\mathcal{M}_{l}\left|\big\{f>l\big\}\cap Q_{R}\right|^{\zeta}.

Step 2-iii: Conclusion.

Since
\phi=1
in
Q_{\frac{R}{8}}
and
\phi=0
outside
Q_{\frac{R}{4}}
, we conclude from (
56
), (
62
), (
63
), (
64
) and (
65
)

(66)

\displaystyle\left\|(f-l)_{+}\phi\right\|_{L^{p}(Q_{\frac{R}{8}})}

\displaystyle\leq CR^{s+\epsilon}\left\|(f-l)_{+}(t_{0})\right\|_{L^{1}(Q^{t_{0}}_{\frac{R}{4}})}+CR^{-s+\epsilon}\left\|(f-l)_{+}\right\|_{L^{1}(Q_{\frac{R}{2}})}

\displaystyle+CR^{-s+\epsilon}\left|\{f>l\}\cap Q_{\frac{R}{2}}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|(f-l)_{+}\right\|_{L^{2}(Q_{\frac{R}{2}})}

\displaystyle+CR^{\frac{n(1-\zeta)}{2}-s+\epsilon}\left(\sup_{Q_{\frac{R}{4}}}(f-l)_{+}\mathcal{M}_{l}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\big\{f>l\big\}\cap Q_{R}\right|^{\frac{\zeta+1}{2}}

\displaystyle+CR^{n(1-\zeta)-s+\epsilon}\mathcal{M}_{l}\left|\big\{f>l\big\}\cap Q_{R}\right|^{\zeta}.

Let us remark that, in case of non-zero source term, we would have the additional term

CR^{s+\epsilon}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R})}\left|\{f>l\}\cap Q_{R}\right|+CR^{\epsilon}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sup_{Q_{R}}(f-l)_{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\{f>l\}\cap Q_{R}\right|

on the right hand side of (
66
).

Step 3: De Giorgi iteration.

Let
L>0
to be determined and consider

l_{k}=L(1-2^{-k}),\quad r_{k}=\frac{R}{8}+2^{-k}\left(R-\frac{R}{8}\right).

Define
Q_{k}=\left(-r_{k}^{2s},0\right]\times B_{r_{k}^{1+2s}}\times B_{r_{k}}
and

A_{k}:=\int_{Q_{k}}(f-l_{k})_{+}(z)\,{\rm d}z.

We want to prove that
A_{k}\to 0
as
k\to+\infty
. This then proves

f(t,x,v)\leq L

for
(t,x,v)\in Q_{\frac{R}{8}}
.

Chebyschev’s inequality gives

(67)

\displaystyle\left|\{f>l_{k+1}\}\cap Q_{k}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left\{(f-l_{k})_{+}>2^{-k-2}L\right\}\cap Q_{k}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2^{k+2}L^{-1}\int_{Q_{k}}(f-l_{k})_{+}(z)\,{\rm d}z=2^{k+2}L^{-1}A_{k}.

To bound the right hand side of (
66
) we pick
t_{k+\frac{1}{2}}\in\left[-r_{k}^{2s},-r_{k+1}^{2s}\right]
such that

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{k}^{t_{k+\frac{1}{2}}}}(f-l_{k})_{+}(t_{k+\frac{1}{2}},x,v)\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{t_{k+1}-t_{k}}\int_{Q_{k}}(f-l_{k})_{+}(t,x,v)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq C2^{k}R^{-2s}A_{k}.

Moreover, we interpolate the
L^{2}
norm between
L^{1}
and
L^{\infty}
,

\displaystyle\left\|(f-l_{k+1})_{+}\right\|_{L^{2}(Q_{k+1})}\leq\left\|(f-l_{k+1})_{+}\right\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}(Q_{k+1})}\left(\sup_{Q_{k+1}}(f-l_{k+1})_{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Then we use the gain of integrability (
66
), Chebyshev (
67
) and
l_{k+1}\geq l_{k}
to find

(68)

\displaystyle\Bigg(\int_{t_{k+\frac{1}{2}}}^{0}

\displaystyle\int_{Q^{t}_{k+1}}(f-l_{k+1})_{+}^{p}(z)\,{\rm d}z\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C2^{4k}R^{-s+\epsilon}A_{k}+C2^{3k}R^{-s+\epsilon}A_{k}

\displaystyle+C2^{4k}R^{-s+\epsilon}L^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sup_{Q_{k+1}}(f-l_{k+1})_{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}A_{k}

\displaystyle+C2^{4k}R^{\frac{n(1-\zeta)}{2}-s+\epsilon}\left(\sup_{Q_{k+1}}(f-l_{k+1})_{+}\mathcal{M}_{l_{k+1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}L^{-\frac{\zeta+1}{2}}A_{k}^{\frac{\zeta+1}{2}}

\displaystyle+C2^{4k}R^{n(1-\zeta)-s+\epsilon}\mathcal{M}_{l_{k+1}}L^{-\zeta}A_{k}^{\zeta}.

Again, for non-zero source term, we have the additional terms

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle 2^{k+2}R^{-s+\epsilon}\left(R^{2s}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{k})}+R^{s}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{k})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sup_{Q_{k}}(f-l_{k})_{+}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)L^{-1}A_{k}

\displaystyle\leq C2^{k+2}R^{-s+\epsilon}\left(2R^{2s}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{k})}+\sup_{Q_{k}}(f-l_{k})_{+}\right)L^{-1}A_{k}

on the right hand side of (
68
).

For arbitrary
\delta\in(0,1]
, pick
L
such that

L\geq\delta\sup_{Q_{R}}f,

or, in case of non-zero source term,

L\geq\delta\left(\sup_{Q_{R}}f+R^{2s}\sup_{Q_{R}}h\right).

Then we bound
\mathcal{M}_{l_{k+1}}
given in (
26
) by

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{l_{k+1}}

\displaystyle\leq\left[1+\left(\frac{\sup_{Q_{R}}f}{L}\right)^{\zeta}+\left(\frac{\sup_{Q_{R}}f}{L}\right)^{1-\zeta}\right]\left(L+\sup_{Q_{R}}f\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left[1+\delta^{-\zeta}+\delta^{-1+\zeta}\right]\left(1+\delta^{-1}\right)L\leq 6\delta^{-1-\zeta}L.

for
\delta\leq 1
and
\zeta\geq\frac{1}{2}
.
Now, using
Q_{k+1}\subset Q_{k}
, Hölder, (
68
) and Chebyschev’s inequality (
67
) we get

\displaystyle A_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\int_{Q_{k+1}}(f-l_{k+1})_{+}(z)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{Q_{k+1}}(f-l_{k+1})^{p}_{+}(z)\,{\rm d}z\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\left|\left\{f>l_{k+1}\right\}\cap Q_{k+1}\right|^{1-\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{t_{k+\frac{1}{2}}}^{0}\int_{Q^{t}_{k+1}}(f-l_{k+1})^{p}_{+}(z)\,{\rm d}z\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\left|\left\{f>l_{k+1}\right\}\cap Q_{k}\right|^{1-\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C2^{4k}R^{-s+\epsilon}\Bigg\{\delta^{-\frac{1}{2}}A_{k}^{1-\frac{\zeta}{2}}+R^{\frac{n(1-\zeta)}{2}}\delta^{-1-\frac{\zeta}{2}}L^{1-\frac{\zeta+1}{2}}A_{k}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+R^{n(1-\zeta)}\delta^{-1-\zeta}L^{1-\zeta}A_{k}^{\frac{\zeta}{2}}\Bigg\}L^{-\frac{p-1}{p}}A_{k}^{\frac{\zeta}{2}+\frac{p-1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C2^{4k}R^{n(1-\zeta)-s+\epsilon}\delta^{-1-\zeta}L^{1-\zeta-\frac{p-1}{p}}A_{k}^{\zeta+\frac{p-1}{p}}.

This estimate holds for any
\zeta\in(0,1)
. We pick
\zeta\in(1-\frac{p-1}{p},1)
as close to
1
as possible.

To determine how large we can pick
L
, we consider the sequence
A_{k}^{*}:=A_{0}Q^{-k}
for some
Q>1
to be determined and for
k\geq 0
. Then we require
A_{k}^{*}
to satisfy the reverse non-linear recurrence, that is

(69)

A_{k+1}^{*}\geq 2^{4k}R^{n(1-\zeta)-s+\epsilon}\delta^{-1-\zeta}L^{1-\zeta-\frac{p-1}{p}}(A_{k}^{*})^{\zeta+\frac{p-1}{p}}.

Equivalently,

1\geq 2^{4k}R^{n(1-\zeta)-s+\epsilon}\delta^{-1-\zeta}L^{1-\zeta-\frac{p-1}{p}}A_{0}^{\zeta-1+\frac{p-1}{p}}Q^{-k(\zeta-1+\frac{p-1}{p})}Q.

Choose
Q
such that
2^{4}Q^{-(\zeta-1+\frac{p-1}{p})}\leq 1
or
2^{4}\leq Q^{\zeta-1+\frac{p-1}{p}}
.
Then (
69
) holds if

L^{\zeta-1+\frac{p-1}{p}}\geq R^{n(1-\zeta)-s+\epsilon}A_{0}^{\zeta-1+\frac{p-1}{p}}Q\delta^{-1-\zeta}.

Therefore, we pick

(70)

L=A_{0}R^{\frac{n(1-\zeta)-(s-\epsilon)}{\zeta-1+\frac{p-1}{p}}}2^{\frac{4}{(\zeta-1+\frac{p-1}{p})^{2}}}\delta^{-\frac{1-\zeta}{\zeta-1+\frac{p-1}{p}}}+\delta\left(\sup_{Q_{R}}f+R^{2s}\sup_{Q_{R}}h\right).

Then since
\frac{p-1}{p}>0
and since
A_{0}\leq 2^{\frac{4}{(\zeta-1+\frac{p-1}{p})^{2}}}R^{\frac{n(1-\zeta)-(s-\epsilon)}{\zeta-1+\frac{p-1}{p}}}\delta^{-\frac{1-\zeta}{\zeta-1+\frac{p-1}{p}}}L
,
we deduce inductively

A_{k}\leq 2^{-\frac{4k}{\zeta-1+\frac{p-1}{p}}}A_{0},

so that
A_{k}\to 0
as
k\to+\infty
.
In particular, for almost every
(t,x,v)\in Q_{\frac{R}{8}}

f(z)\leq L=\delta\left(\sup_{Q_{R}}f+R^{2s}\sup_{Q_{R}}h\right)+2^{\frac{4}{(\zeta-1+\frac{p-1}{p})^{2}}}\delta^{-\frac{1-\zeta}{\zeta-1+\frac{p-1}{p}}}R^{\frac{n(1-\zeta)-(s-\epsilon)}{\zeta-1+\frac{p-1}{p}}}\int_{Q_{R}}f(z)\,{\rm d}z.

Recall that we pick
\zeta
arbitrarily close to
1
, and
\epsilon
arbitrarily close to
0
.
Finally, since
\sup_{Q_{R}}f<+\infty
(due to the non-local
L^{2}
-
L^{\infty}
estimate as established in
[
22
, Lemma 6.6]
, or
[
35
, Lemma 4.1]
together with Definition
1
), so that we can absorb the first term on the left hand side with Lemma
10
.
This concludes the proof of Proposition
5
. Remarkably, the proof only works for solutions, and not for sub-solutions, which is in contrast to the local case.

5.
Strong Harnack inequality

Proof of the Strong Harnack inequality in Theorem
1
.

As a consequence of Proposition
5
, Young’s inequality, the non-negativity of
f
and the Weak Harnack (
23
), we obtain for any
\delta_{1}\in(0,1)

\displaystyle\sup_{Q_{\frac{R}{8}}(z_{0})}f

\displaystyle\leq CR^{-(2d(1+s)+2s)}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{1}(Q_{R}(z_{0}))}+CR^{2s}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{1}\sup_{Q_{R}(z_{0})}f+C(\delta_{1})R^{-\frac{2d(1+s)+2s}{\zeta}}\Bigg(\int_{Q_{R}(z_{0})}f^{\zeta}\,{\rm d}z\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{\zeta}}+CR^{2s}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R}(z_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{1}\sup_{Q_{R}(z_{0})}f+C(\delta_{1})R^{-\frac{2d(1+s)+2s}{\zeta}}\inf_{\tilde{Q}_{R}^{+}(z_{0})}f+CR^{-\frac{2d(1+s)+2s}{\zeta}+2s}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R}(z_{0}))},

where we recall
\tilde{Q}_{R}^{+}:=Q_{R}\left(\left(\frac{3}{2}R^{2s}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{R}{2}\right)^{2s},0,0\right)\right)
.
Lemma
10
permits to absorb the first term on the right hand side, and we conclude

\sup_{Q_{\frac{R}{8}}(z_{0})}f\leq CR^{-\frac{2d(1+s)+2s}{\zeta}}\left(\inf_{\tilde{Q}_{R}^{+}(z_{0})}f+R^{2s}\left\|h\right\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R}(z_{0}))}\right).

∎

6.
Upper bound on the fundamental solution

For the sequel we assume that (
1
) under assumptions (
15
)-(
18
) admits a fundamental solution with the properties (
4
), (
5
) and (
6
). We denote it by
J(t,x,v;\tau,\zeta,\xi)
for
(t,x,v),(\tau,\zeta,\xi)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1+2d}
.

6.1.
On-diagonal bound

The on-diagonal upper bound follows from Proposition
5
.

Theorem 14

(Nash Upper Bound)
.

Let
x,v,\zeta,\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
0\leq\tau_{0}<t<T
. Let
J(t,x,v;\tau_{0},\zeta,\xi)
be the fundamental solution of (
1
) with a non-negative kernel
K
satisfying (
15
)-(
17
). Then there exists
C>0
depending on
s,d,\lambda_{0},\Lambda_{0}
such that

(71)

J(t,x,v;\tau_{0},\zeta,\xi)\leq C(t-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{2d(1+s)}{2s}}.

Proof.

Since
(t,x,v)\to J(t,x,v;\tau_{0},\zeta,\xi)
solves (
1
), we find by Proposition
5
with
z=(t,x,v)
,
r^{2s}=\frac{1}{4}(t-\tau_{0})
and
R=2r

\displaystyle J(t,x,v;\tau_{0},\zeta,\xi)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{Q_{r}(z)}J(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot;\tau_{0},\zeta,\xi)

\displaystyle\leq C(t-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{2d(1+s)+2s}{2s}}\int_{Q_{2r}(z)}J(s,y,w;\tau_{0},\zeta,\xi)\,{\rm d}s\,{\rm d}y\,{\rm d}w

\displaystyle\leq C(t-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{2d(1+s)+2s}{2s}}\int_{t-r^{2s}}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}J(s,y,w;\tau_{0},\zeta,\xi)\,{\rm d}s\,{\rm d}y\,{\rm d}w

\displaystyle\leq C(t-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{2d(1+s)}{2s}}.

The last inequality follows by using (
4
).
∎

As a consequence of (
71
) and (
4
) we get for every
0\leq\tau_{0}<\sigma<T
and
\zeta,\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}

(72)

\Bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}J^{2}(\sigma,x,v;\tau_{0},\zeta,\xi)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{d(1+s)}{2s}},

and due to (
5
) also for every
x,v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}

(73)

\Bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}J^{2}(\sigma,x,v;\tau_{0},\zeta,\xi)\,{\rm d}\zeta\,{\rm d}\xi\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{d(1+s)}{2s}}.

6.2.
Off-diagonal bound

In this section, we derive an off-diagonal bound on the fundamental solution.

Theorem 15

(Off-diagonal bound)
.

Let
J
be the fundamental solution of (
1
) with a non-negative kernel
K
satisfying (
15
)-(
18
). Then there exists
C>0
depending on
d,\lambda_{0},\Lambda_{0},s
such that for every
0<\rho
,
0\leq\tau_{0}<\sigma<T
,
x,v,y_{0},w_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
with
\sigma-\tau_{0}\leq\frac{\rho^{2s}}{4k}
there holds

J(\sigma,x,v;\tau_{0},y_{0},w_{0})\leq C\Bigg((\sigma-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{2d(1+s)}{2s}}\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\gamma}{12\rho}}+\rho^{-\frac{s}{2}}(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{2d(1+s)}{2s}}\Bigg),

for

\gamma=\frac{1}{4}\max\left\{\left|v-w_{0}\right|,\left|x-y_{0}-(\sigma-\tau_{0})w_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}\right\},

with
\left|\sigma-\tau_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{2s}}\leq 4\gamma
.

To prove Theorem
15
, we use Aronson’s method: we construct a function
H
that satisfies

\mathcal{T}H+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\left[H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)-H^{\frac{1}{2}}(w)\right]^{2}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\leq 0.

Testing (
1
) with the product of the solution and
H
then yields

(74)

\sup_{t\in(t_{0},\sigma)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f^{2}(t,x,v)H(t,x,v)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v\lesssim\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f^{2}(\tau_{0},x,v)H(\tau_{0},x,v)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v.

The decay from the function
H
determines through this inequality (
74
) how quick solutions increase inside a ball that lies outside the support of the initial data:

Theorem 16

(Aronson’s bound)
.

Let
0<\tau_{0}<\sigma<T
,
0<\rho
,
\gamma>0
such that
\left|\sigma-\tau_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{2s}}\leq\gamma
, and
(y_{0},w_{0})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
. Let
f_{0}=f(\tau_{0})\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
be such that

f(t_{0},x,v)=0,\qquad\textrm{ for }\max\left\{\left|x-y_{0}-(\sigma-\tau_{0})w_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}},\left|v-w_{0}\right|\right\}\leq\gamma.

Let
f\in L^{\infty}\big((\tau_{0},T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}\big)
solve (
1
) in
(\tau_{0},T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
with a non-local operator whose kernel is non-negative and satisfies (
15
), (
16
), (
17
), (
18
).
Then there exists
k\geq 1
, and
C>0
depending on
s,d,\lambda_{0},\Lambda_{0}
such that if
\sigma-\tau_{0}<\frac{\rho^{2s}}{4k}
there holds

\left|f(\sigma,y_{0},w_{0})\right|\leq C\Bigg[(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{d(1+s)}{2s}}\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\gamma}{6\rho}}+\rho^{-s}(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{d(1+s)}{2s}}\Bigg]\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}.

Before proving Theorem
16
, we complete the proof of the off-diagonal bound in Theorem
15
using Theorem
16
and the on-diagonal bounds in Theorem
14
.

Proof of Theorem
15
.

Step 1: First consequence of Theorem
16
.

Let
0<\rho
, and
0\leq\tau_{0}<\sigma
be such that
\sigma-\tau_{0}\leq\frac{\rho^{2s}}{4k}
. Let
v,\xi,x,\zeta\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
. Then we claim that for
0\leq\tau_{0}<t<\sigma
there holds

(75)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}\setminus Q^{\tau_{0}}_{\gamma}(t,x,v)}J^{2}(t,x,v;\tau_{0},\zeta,\xi)\,{\rm d}\zeta\,{\rm d}\xi

\displaystyle\leq C(t-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{2d(1+s)}{2s}}\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\gamma}{6\rho}}+\rho^{-s}(t-\tau_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2d(1+s)}{2s}},\qquad x,v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}\setminus Q^{t}_{\gamma}(\tau_{0},\zeta,\xi)}J^{2}(t,x,v;\tau_{0},\zeta,\xi)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq C(t-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{2d(1+s)}{2s}}\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\gamma}{6\rho}}+\rho^{-s}(t-\tau_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2d(1+s)}{2s}},\qquad\zeta,\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},

where

Q^{\tau_{0}}_{\gamma}(t,x,v):=\left\{(\zeta,\xi)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}:\max\left\{\left|\zeta-x-(t-\tau_{0})v\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}},\left|\xi-v\right|\right\}<\gamma\right\},

and similarly,

Q^{t}_{\gamma}(\tau_{0},\zeta,\xi):=\left\{(x,v)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}:\max\left\{\left|x-\zeta-(\tau_{0}-t)\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}},\left|v-\xi\right|\right\}<\gamma\right\}.

To prove this, we first note

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\gamma}^{\tau_{0}}(t,x,v)}J^{2}(t,x,v;\tau_{0},\zeta,\xi)\,{\rm d}\zeta\,{\rm d}\xi=\int_{Q_{\gamma}^{0}(0,y,w)}J^{2}(t,y-(t-\tau_{0})w,w;\tau_{0},\zeta,\xi)\,{\rm d}\zeta\,{\rm d}\xi.

Then we define

f(\sigma,y_{0},w_{0}):=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}\setminus Q_{\gamma}^{0}(0,y,w)}J(\sigma,y_{0},w_{0};\tau_{0},\zeta,\xi)J(t,y-(t-\tau_{0})w,w;\tau_{0},\zeta,\xi)\,{\rm d}\zeta\,{\rm d}\xi.

This function
f
is a non-negative solution of (
1
) for
0\leq\tau_{0}<\sigma<T
with initial condition

\displaystyle f(\tau_{0},y_{0},w_{0})=\begin{cases}0,&\qquad\textrm{ if }(y_{0},w_{0})\in Q_{\gamma}^{0}(0,y,w),\\
J(t,y-(t-\tau_{0})w,w;\tau_{0},y_{0},w_{0}),&\qquad\textrm{ if }(y_{0},w_{0})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}\setminus Q_{\gamma}^{0}(0,y,w),\end{cases}

due to (
6
) and (
5
).
Setting
(\sigma,y_{0},w_{0})=(t,y-(t-\tau_{0})w,w)
yields by Theorem
16

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}\setminus Q_{\gamma}^{0}(0,y,w)}J^{2}(t,y-(t-\tau_{0})w,w;\tau_{0},\zeta,\xi)\,{\rm d}\zeta\,{\rm d}\xi

\displaystyle=f(\sigma,y-(t-\tau_{0})w,w)

\displaystyle\leq C(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{d(1+s)}{2s}}\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\gamma}{6\rho}}\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}+\rho^{-s}(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{d(1+s)}{2s}}\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}

\displaystyle\leq C(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{d(1+s)}{2s}}\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\gamma}{6\rho}}\left\|J(t,y-(t-\tau_{0})w,w;\tau_{0},\cdot,\cdot)\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}

\displaystyle+\rho^{-s}(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{d(1+s)}{2s}}\left\|J(t,y-(t-\tau_{0})w,w;\tau_{0},\cdot,\cdot)\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}

\displaystyle\leq C(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{2d(1+s)}{2s}}\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\gamma}{6\rho}}+\rho^{-s}(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2d(1+s)}{2s}}.

The last inequality follows from the on-diagonal bound (
73
). This yields the first inequality in claim (
75
). Together with (
5
) this also yields the second inequality in (
75
).

Step 2: Second consequence of Theorem
16
.

We fix
0\leq\tau_{0}<\sigma<T
such that
\sigma-\tau_{0}\leq\frac{\rho^{2s}}{4k}
and
x,v,y_{0},w_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
. We consider

(76)

\gamma=\frac{1}{4}\max\left\{\left|v-w_{0}\right|,\left|x-y_{0}-(\sigma-\tau_{0})w_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}\right\},

and assume
\left|\sigma-\tau_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{2s}}\leq 4\gamma
.
Then by (
6
)

\displaystyle J

\displaystyle(\sigma,x,v;\tau_{0},y_{0},w_{0})

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}J\Big(\sigma,x,v;\sigma-\frac{\sigma-\tau_{0}}{2},x^{\prime},v^{\prime}\Big)J\Big(\sigma-\frac{\sigma-\tau_{0}}{2},x^{\prime},v^{\prime};\tau_{0},y_{0},w_{0}\Big)\,{\rm d}x^{\prime}\,{\rm d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}\setminus Q^{\frac{\sigma-\tau_{0}}{2}}_{\gamma}(0,x,v)}\dots\,{\rm d}x^{\prime}\,{\rm d}v^{\prime}}_{=:J_{1}}+\underbrace{\int_{Q_{\gamma}^{\frac{\sigma-\tau_{0}}{2}}(0,x,v)}\dots\,{\rm d}x^{\prime}\,{\rm d}v^{\prime}}_{=:J_{2}}.

For
J_{1}
we use (
72
) and (
75
) to get

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}\setminus Q^{\frac{\sigma-\tau_{0}}{2}}_{\gamma}(0,x,v)}J^{2}\Big(\sigma,x,v;\sigma-\frac{\sigma-\tau_{0}}{2},x^{\prime},v^{\prime}\Big)\,{\rm d}x^{\prime}\,{\rm d}v^{\prime}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\Bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}\setminus Q^{\frac{\sigma-\tau_{0}}{2}}_{\gamma}(0,x,v)}J^{2}\Big(\sigma-\frac{\sigma-\tau_{0}}{2},x^{\prime},v^{\prime};\tau_{0},y_{0},w_{0}\Big)\,{\rm d}x^{\prime}\,{\rm d}v^{\prime}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg((\sigma-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{2d(1+s)}{4s}}\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\gamma}{12\rho}}+\rho^{-\frac{s}{2}}(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{2d(1+s)}{4s}}\Bigg)(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{2d(1+s)}{4s}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg((\sigma-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{2d(1+s)}{2s}}\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\gamma}{12\rho}}+\rho^{-\frac{s}{2}}(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{2d(1+s)}{2s}}\Bigg).

For
J_{2}
we first note that for
x^{\prime},v^{\prime}\in Q_{\gamma}^{\frac{\sigma-\tau_{0}}{2}}(0,x,v)
by choice of
\gamma
in (
76
) either

\displaystyle\left|v^{\prime}-w_{0}\right|\geq\left|v-w_{0}\right|-\left|v^{\prime}-v\right|\geq 4\gamma-\gamma\geq\gamma,

or

\displaystyle(4\gamma)^{1+2s}

\displaystyle=\left|x-y_{0}-(\sigma-\tau_{0})w_{0}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left(x-x^{\prime}-\frac{(\sigma-\tau_{0})}{2}v\right)+\left(x^{\prime}-y_{0}-\frac{(\sigma-\tau_{0})}{2}w_{0}\right)+\frac{(\sigma-\tau_{0})}{2}(v-w_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\gamma^{1+2s}+\left|x^{\prime}-y_{0}-\frac{(\sigma-\tau_{0})}{2}w_{0}\right|+\frac{1}{2}4^{2s}\gamma^{2s}4\gamma\right|

\displaystyle\leq(1+2^{1+4s})\gamma^{1+2s}+\left|x^{\prime}-y_{0}-\frac{(\sigma-\tau_{0})}{2}w_{0}\right|,

since
x^{\prime}\in Q_{\gamma}^{\frac{\sigma-\tau_{0}}{2}}(0,x,v)
and by choice of
\gamma
in (
76
), so that

\displaystyle\left|x^{\prime}-y_{0}-\frac{(\sigma-\tau)}{2}w_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}\geq(4^{1+2s}-(1+2^{1+4s}))^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}\gamma\geq\gamma.

In other words,
Q_{\gamma}^{\frac{\sigma-\tau_{0}}{2}}(0,x,v)\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}\setminus Q_{\gamma}^{\frac{\sigma-\tau_{0}}{2}}(0,y_{0},w_{0})
so that again by (
72
) and (
75
) we get

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}\setminus Q^{\frac{\sigma-\tau_{0}}{2}}_{\gamma}(0,y_{0},w_{0})}J^{2}\left(\sigma,x,v;\sigma-\frac{\sigma-\tau_{0}}{2},x^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\,{\rm d}x^{\prime}\,{\rm d}v^{\prime}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\Bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}\setminus Q^{\frac{\sigma-\tau_{0}}{2}}_{\gamma}(0,y_{0},w_{0})}J^{2}\left(\sigma-\frac{\sigma-\tau_{0}}{2},x^{\prime},v^{\prime};\tau_{0},y_{0},w_{0}\right)\,{\rm d}x^{\prime}\,{\rm d}v^{\prime}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg((\sigma-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{2d(1+s)}{4s}}\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\gamma}{12\rho}}+\rho^{-\frac{s}{2}}(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{2d(1+s)}{4s}}\Bigg)(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{2d(1+s)}{4s}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg((\sigma-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{2d(1+s)}{2s}}\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\gamma}{12\rho}}+\rho^{-\frac{s}{2}}(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{2d(1+s)}{2s}}\Bigg),

which proves Theorem
15
.
∎

It remains to establish Aronson’s method.

6.3.
Aronson’s method

The proof of Theorem
16
relies on Aronson’s method: we aim to construct a suitable function
H
to derive (
74
).

6.3.1.
Decay relation

We aim to define a function that satisfies for some
\rho>0
and for some constant
c>0

(77)

\mathcal{T}H(v)+c\underbrace{\int_{B_{\rho}(v)}\Big[H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)-H^{\frac{1}{2}}(w)\Big]^{2}\big[K(v,w)+K(w,v)\big]\,{\rm d}w}_{=:\mathcal{L}_{\rho}H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq 0.

Then we can derive the following statement.

Proposition 17
.

Let
0<\tau_{0}<\sigma<T
and
0<\rho
.
Let
f\in L^{\infty}\big((\tau_{0},T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}\big)
solve (
1
) in
(\tau_{0},T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
with a non-local operator whose kernel is non-negative and satisfies (
16
), (
17
), (
18
) (if
s\geq\frac{1}{2}
). Then for every bounded function
H:[\tau_{0},\sigma]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}\to[0,\infty)
such that
H^{\frac{1}{2}}\in L^{2}\big((\tau_{0},\sigma)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d};H^{s}_{v}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\big)

satisfies (
77
) in
(\tau_{0},\sigma)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
,
there exists a constant
C=C(\Lambda_{0},s,d)
such that

(78)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(\tau_{0},\sigma)}

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f^{2}(t,x,v)H(t,x,v)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f^{2}(\tau_{0},x,v)H(\tau_{0},x,v)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+C\rho^{-2s}\left\|H\right\|_{L^{\infty}([\tau_{0},\sigma]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}([\tau_{0},\sigma]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}^{2}.

Proof of Proposition
17
.

For
R\geq\max\{2,2\rho+1\}
we consider
\varphi_{R}\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
with
0\leq\varphi_{R}\leq 1
such that
\varphi_{R}\equiv 1
for
(x,v)\in B_{(R-1)^{1+2s}}\times B_{R-1}
and
\varphi_{R}\equiv 0
for
(x,v)
outside
B_{R^{1+2s}}\times B_{R}
, with bounded derivatives and such that
\left|v\cdot\varphi_{R}\right|\sim R^{-2s}
. We test (
1
) with
fH\varphi_{R}^{2}
where
H
satisfies (
77
) over
[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
for
0\leq\tau_{0}\leq\tau_{1}\leq\sigma
and get

\displaystyle 0=\int_{[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}\mathcal{T}f

\displaystyle\Big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\Big)\,{\rm d}z-\int_{[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}\mathcal{L}f\Big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\Big)\,{\rm d}z.

Step 1: Transport operator.

First we integrate by parts the transport operator

(79)

\displaystyle\int_{[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}\mathcal{T}f\Big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\Big)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f^{2}H\varphi_{R}^{2}\Big|_{t=\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v-\int_{[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f^{2}H\varphi_{R}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{R}\,{\rm d}z-\frac{1}{2}\int_{[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f^{2}\varphi_{R}^{2}\mathcal{T}H\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f^{2}H\varphi_{R}^{2}\Big|_{t=\tau_{0}}^{\tau_{1}}\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v-CR^{-2s}\int_{[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f^{2}H\varphi_{R}\,{\rm d}z-\frac{1}{2}\int_{[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f^{2}\varphi_{R}^{2}\mathcal{T}H\,{\rm d}z.

Step 2: Non-local operator.

Now we deal with the non-local term.
We write

(80)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{L}f\big)fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{B_{2R}}\int_{B_{2R}}\big[f(w)-f(v)\big]\Big[\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(v)-\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(w)\Big]K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{B_{2R}}\int_{B_{2R}}\big[f(w)-f(v)\big]\Big[\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(v)+\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(w)\Big]K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{2R}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{2R}}\big[f(w)-f(v)\big]\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(v)K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{B_{2R}}\int_{B_{2R}}\big[f(w)-f(v)\big]\Big[\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(v)-\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(w)\Big]K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{2R}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{2R}}\big[f(w)-f(v)\big]\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(v)K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle=:\mathcal{I}_{loc}+\mathcal{I}_{tail},

where we used the divergence structure (
17
).

Step 2-i: Tail.

First we note that the tail tends to zero as
R\to\infty
:

(81)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{tail}

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{2R}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{2R}}\big[f(w)-f(v)\big]\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(v)K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{R}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{2R}}f(w)\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(v)K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq C\Lambda R^{-2s}\left\|H\varphi_{R}^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{1}(B_{R})}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{2R})}\xrightarrow[R\to\infty]{}0.

Step 2-ii: Symmetric not-too-non-local non-locality.

Second, to bound
\mathcal{I}_{loc}
from (
80
), we first distinguish the non-singular from the singular part. We write

(82)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{loc}

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{2R}}\int_{B_{2R}}\big[f(w)-f(v)\big]\Big[\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(v)-\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(w)\Big]K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{2R}}\int_{B_{2R}}\big[f(w)-f(v)\big]\Big[\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(v)-\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(w)\Big]K(v,w)\chi_{\left|v-w\right|>\rho}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{2R}}\int_{B_{2R}}\big[f(w)-f(v)\big]\Big[\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(v)-\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(w)\Big]K(v,w)\chi_{\left|v-w\right|<\rho}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle=:\mathcal{I}_{loc}^{ns}+\mathcal{I}_{loc}^{s}.

Then, on the one hand, we crudely bound with (
16
)

(83)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{loc}^{ns}

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{2R}}\int_{B_{2R}}\big[f(w)-f(v)\big]\Big[\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(v)-\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(w)\Big]K(v,w)\chi_{\left|v-w\right|>\rho}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{2R}}\int_{B_{2R}}\Big[f(w)\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(v)+f(v)\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(w)\Big]K(v,w)\chi_{\left|v-w\right|>\rho}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{2R}}\int_{B_{2R}}\big[f^{2}(w)+f^{2}(v)\big]\big(H\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(v)+\big(H\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(w)\big]K(v,w)\chi_{\left|v-w\right|>\rho}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq C\rho^{-2s}\left\|H\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{B_{2R}}f^{2}(v)\,{\rm d}v.

On the other hand, to bound the singular part of
\mathcal{I}_{loc}
, we realise

\displaystyle\big[f(v)

\displaystyle-f(w)\big]\Big[\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(v)-\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(w)\Big]

\displaystyle=\Big[\big(fH^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi_{R}\big)(v)-\big(fH^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi_{R}\big)(w)\Big]^{2}-f(v)f(w)\Big[\big(\varphi_{R}H^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)(v)-\big(\varphi_{R}H^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)(w)\Big]^{2},

so that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{loc}^{s}

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{2R}}\int_{B_{2R}}f(v)f(w)\Big[\big(\varphi_{R}H^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)(v)-\big(\varphi_{R}H^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)(w)\Big]^{2}K(v,w)\chi_{\left|v-w\right|<\rho}\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}w

\displaystyle-\int_{B_{2R}}\int_{B_{2R}}\Big[\big(fH^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi_{R}\big)(v)-\big(fH^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi_{R}\big)(w)\Big]^{2}K(v,w)\chi_{\left|v-w\right|<\rho}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v.

Now notice that for some
c_{0}\geq 1
there holds

\displaystyle f(v)

\displaystyle f(w)\Big[\big(\varphi_{R}H^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)(v)-\big(\varphi_{R}H^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)(w)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c_{0}\Big[f^{2}(v)+f^{2}(w)\Big]\Big[\big(\varphi_{R}(v)-\varphi_{R}(w)\big)^{2}\big(H(v)+H(w)\big)+\Big(H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)-H^{\frac{1}{2}}(w)\Big)^{2}\Big(\varphi_{R}^{2}(v)+\varphi_{R}^{2}(w)\Big)\Big].

Thus

(84)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{loc}^{s}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{2R}}\int_{B_{2R}}f(v)f(w)\Big[\big(\varphi_{R}H^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)(v)-\big(\varphi_{R}H^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)(w)\Big]^{2}K(v,w)\chi_{\left|v-w\right|<\rho}\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}w

\displaystyle\lesssim_{c_{0}}\underbrace{\int_{B_{2R}}\int_{B_{2R}}\big[f^{2}(v)+f^{2}(w)\big]\big[\varphi_{R}(v)-\varphi_{R}(w)\big]^{2}\big[H(v)+H(w)\big]K(v,w)\chi_{\left|v-w\right|<\rho}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v}_{=:\mathcal{I}_{loc}^{s,\varphi}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\int_{B_{2R}}\int_{B_{2R}}\big[f^{2}(v)+f^{2}(w)\big]\Big[H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)-H^{\frac{1}{2}}(w)\Big]^{2}\Big[\varphi_{R}^{2}(v)+\varphi_{R}^{2}(w)\Big]K(v,w)\chi_{\left|v-w\right|<\rho}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v}_{=:\mathcal{I}_{loc}^{s,H}}.

We start with
\mathcal{I}_{loc}^{s,\varphi}
: we observe that
\varphi_{R}(v)=\varphi_{R}(w)
for
w\in B_{\rho}(v)
with
v\in B_{\frac{R-1}{2}}
. Thus

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{loc}^{s,\varphi}

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{\frac{R-1}{2}}}\int_{B_{2R}\cap B_{\rho}(v)}\big[f^{2}(v)+f^{2}(w)\big]\big[\varphi_{R}(v)-\varphi_{R}(w)\big]^{2}\big[H(v)+H(w)\big]K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{2R}\setminus B_{\frac{R-1}{2}}}\int_{B_{2R}\cap B_{\rho}(v)}\big[f^{2}(v)+f^{2}(w)\big]\big[\varphi_{R}(v)-\varphi_{R}(w)\big]^{2}\big[H(v)+H(w)\big]K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{2R}\setminus B_{\frac{R-1}{2}}}\int_{B_{2R}\cap B_{\rho}(v)}\big[f^{2}(v)+f^{2}(w)\big]\big[\varphi_{R}(v)-\varphi_{R}(w)\big]^{2}\big[H(v)+H(w)\big]K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v.

Since the integrand is bounded, we obtain as
R\to\infty

(85)

\mathcal{I}_{loc}^{s,\varphi}\to 0.

We continue with
\mathcal{I}_{loc}^{s,H}
: due to (
17
), the integrand is symmetric, so that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{I}_{loc}^{s,H}

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{2R}}\int_{B_{2R}}f^{2}(v)\big[\varphi_{R}^{2}(v)+\varphi_{R}^{2}(w)\big]\Big[H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)-H^{\frac{1}{2}}(w)\Big]^{2}K(v,w)\chi_{\left|v-w\right|<\rho}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{\frac{R-1}{2}}}\int_{B_{2R}\cap B_{\rho}(v)}f^{2}(v)\big[\varphi_{R}^{2}(v)+\varphi_{R}^{2}(w)\big]\Big[H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)-H^{\frac{1}{2}}(w)\Big]^{2}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{2R}\setminus B_{\frac{R-1}{2}}}\int_{B_{2R}\cap B_{\rho}(v)}f^{2}(v)\big[\varphi_{R}^{2}(v)+\varphi_{R}^{2}(w)\big]\Big[H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)-H^{\frac{1}{2}}(w)\Big]^{2}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle=:\mathcal{I}_{loc}^{H-decay}+\mathcal{I}_{loc}^{H-error}.

The first term encodes the localised decay of
H
. The second term tends to zero as
R\to\infty
, as the integrand is bounded.
Concretely, since
\varphi_{R}(v)=\varphi_{R}(w)
for any
w\in B_{\rho}(v)
and
v\in B_{\frac{R-1}{2}}
if
R\geq 1+2\rho
, we find

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{loc}^{H-decay}=2\int_{B_{\frac{R-1}{2}}}\int_{B_{2R}\cap B_{\rho}(v)}f^{2}(v)\varphi_{R}^{2}(v)\Big[H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)-H^{\frac{1}{2}}(w)\Big]^{2}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v.

For the second term, we see that the integrand is bounded so that

\mathcal{I}_{loc}^{H-error}\leq 2\int_{B_{2R}\setminus B_{\frac{R-1}{2}}}\int_{B_{2R}\cap B_{\rho}(v)}f^{2}(v)\Big[H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)-H^{\frac{1}{2}}(w)\Big]^{2}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v\xrightarrow[R\to\infty]{}0.

Thus, as
R\to\infty
, we get

(86)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{loc}^{s,H}\leq

\displaystyle 4\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{B_{\rho}(v)}f^{2}(v)\varphi_{R}^{2}(v)\Big[H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)-H^{\frac{1}{2}}(w)\Big]^{2}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v.

The combination of (
82
) with (
83
), (
84
), (
85
) and (
86
) yields as
R\to\infty

(87)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{loc}\leq

\displaystyle 2\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{B_{\rho}(v)}f^{2}(v)\Big[H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)-H^{\frac{1}{2}}(w)\Big]^{2}\big[K(v,w)+K(w,v)\big]\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+C\rho^{-2s}\left\|H\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{2}.

Therefore, by letting
R\to\infty
, we conclude from (
80
), (
81
), (
87
)

(88)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big(\mathcal{L}f\big)fH\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq 2c_{0}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{B_{\rho}(v)}f^{2}(v)\Big[H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)-H^{\frac{1}{2}}(w)\Big]^{2}\big[K(v,w)+K(w,v)\big]\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+C\rho^{-2s}\left\|H\right\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{2}.

Step 3: Conclusion.

We assemble the pieces. Equation (
79
) implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f^{2}(\tau_{1},x,v)H(\tau_{1},x,v)\varphi_{R}^{2}\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f^{2}(\tau_{0},x,v)H(\tau_{0},x,v)\varphi_{R}^{2}\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+CR^{-2s}\int_{[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f^{2}(z)H(z)\varphi_{R}(x,v)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle+\int_{[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f^{2}(z)\varphi_{R}^{2}(x,v)\mathcal{T}H(z)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle+\int_{[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}\big(\mathcal{L}f\big)(z)f(z)H(z)\varphi_{R}^{2}(x,v)\,{\rm d}z,

which as
R\to\infty
yields together with (
88
)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f^{2}(\tau_{1},x,v)H(\tau_{1},x,v)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f^{2}(\tau_{0},x,v)H(\tau_{0},x,v)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v+\int_{[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f^{2}(z)\mathcal{T}H(z)\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle+2c_{0}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{B_{\rho}(v)}f^{2}(v)\Big[H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)-H^{\frac{1}{2}}(w)\Big]^{2}\big[K(v,w)+K(w,v)\big]\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+C\rho^{-2s}\left\|H\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}([\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f^{2}(\tau_{0},x,v)H(\tau_{0},x,v)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v+C\rho^{-2s}\left\|H\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}([\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}^{2},

since by construction
H
satisfies (
77
) with
c=2c_{0}
. This concludes the proof of Proposition
17
.
∎

6.3.2.
Construction of
H
.

In this section, we construct a function
H
that satisfies (
77
).

Lemma 18
.

Let
y_{0},w_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
. Let
0<\rho
and
0\leq\tau_{0}<\sigma
. Let
k\geq 1
be such that
\sigma-\tau_{0}\leq\frac{\rho^{2s}}{4k}
.
For
(t,x,v)\in[\tau_{0},\sigma]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
, we define
\delta(t):=2(\sigma-\tau_{0})-(t-\tau_{0})
and

(89)

H(t,x,v):=e^{-\max\Big\{1,\frac{1}{3\rho}\max\Big(\left|v-w_{0}\right|,\left|x-y_{0}-(\sigma+t-2\tau_{0})w_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}\Big)\Big\}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)}.

Then there exist a constant
C_{1}>0
depending only on
s,d,\lambda_{0},\Lambda_{0}
such that, if
k>C_{1}
, then
H
satisfies (
77
), where
K
is a non-negative kernel satisfying (
16
), (
17
), (
18
) (if
s\geq\frac{1}{2}
).

Proof.

We verify that
H
satisfies (
77
) by a case distinction.

We abbreviate in the sequel

\left|X-Y_{0}\right|:=\left|x-y_{0}-(\sigma+t-2\tau_{0})w_{0}\right|,

for readability.

i.

For
(t,x,v)
such that
\left|v-w_{0}\right|<2\rho
and
\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}<3\rho
:
then

H(t,x,v)=\frac{k\delta(t)}{\rho^{2s}},

so that

\partial_{t}H=-\frac{k}{\rho^{2s}},\qquad v\cdot\nabla_{x}H=0,\qquad\mathcal{L}_{\rho}H^{\frac{1}{2}}=0.

Thus for any
k>0
(
77
) is satisfied.

ii.

For
(t,x,v)
such that
\left|v-w_{0}\right|<2\rho
and
\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}>3\rho
.
Then

H(t,x,v)=e^{-\frac{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}}{3\rho}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)},

so that

\mathcal{L}_{\rho}H^{\frac{1}{2}}=0,

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}H(t,x,v)

\displaystyle=-\Bigg[\frac{1}{3\rho(1+2s)}\log\Big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\Big)\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{-\frac{1+4s}{1+2s}}(X-Y_{0})\cdot(v-w_{0})

\displaystyle+\frac{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}}{3\rho\delta(t)}\Bigg]H(t,x,v)

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg[\frac{\rho^{2s}}{3\rho k\delta(t)}(3\rho)^{-2s}(2\rho)-\frac{1}{\delta(t)}\Bigg]e^{-\frac{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}}{3\rho}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)}

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg[\frac{1}{k\delta(t)}-\frac{1}{\delta(t)}\Bigg]e^{-\frac{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}}{3\rho}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)}\leq 0,

for any
k\geq 1
. We used the bounds
\left|v-w_{0}\right|<2\rho,\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}>3\rho
, and the fact that
\log a<a
for
a>0
.

iii.

For
(t,x,v)
such that
2\rho<\left|v-w_{0}\right|<3\rho
and
\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}<3\rho
.
Then

H(t,x,v)=\frac{k\delta(t)}{\rho^{2s}},

but for
w\in B_{\rho}(v)
it might happen that
\left|w-w_{0}\right|>3\rho
, in which case

H(t,x,w)=e^{-\frac{1}{3\rho}\left|w-w_{0}\right|\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta}\big)}\leq\frac{k\delta(t)}{\rho^{2s}}=H(t,x,v),

with

\nabla_{v}H^{\frac{1}{2}}(t,x,w)=-\frac{1}{6\rho}\log\Big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta}\Big)\frac{(w-w_{0})}{\left|w-w_{0}\right|}e^{-\frac{1}{6\rho}\left|w-w_{0}\right|\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta}\big)}.

We fix
v_{0}\in B_{\rho}(v)
with
\left|v_{0}-w_{0}\right|=3\rho
such that for every
w\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{3\rho}(w_{0})
there holds
\left|v_{0}-w\right|\leq 2\left|v-w\right|
. As Kassmann-Weidner
[
28
]
point out, this works for example if
v_{0}\in\partial B_{3\rho}(w_{0})
so that
v_{0}
minimises
\textrm{dist}(v,{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{3\rho}(w_{0}))
.
Then
B_{\rho}(v)\subset B_{2\rho}(v_{0})
and
H(t,x,v_{0})=H(t,x,v)
.
Note that

\left|H^{\frac{1}{2}}(t,x,v_{0})-H^{\frac{1}{2}}(t,x,w)\right|^{2}\leq\left|\nabla_{v}H^{\frac{1}{2}}(t,x,v_{0})\right|^{2}\left|v_{0}-w\right|^{2}

since

\sup_{w\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{3\rho}(w_{0})}\left|\nabla_{v}H^{\frac{1}{2}}(t,x,w)\right|=\left|\nabla_{v}H^{\frac{1}{2}}(t,x,v_{0})\right|,\quad\textrm{ for }v_{0}\in\partial B_{3\rho}(w_{0}).

Therefore, using the upper bound (
16
) and
\left|v_{0}-w_{0}\right|=3\rho
, as well as
\log a\leq a^{1/2}
, we get

(90)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\rho}H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{\rho}(v)}\left(H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)-H^{\frac{1}{2}}(w)\right)^{2}\big(K(v,w)+K(w,v)\big)\,{\rm d}w

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{\rho}(v)\setminus B_{3\rho}(w_{0})}\left(H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)-H^{\frac{1}{2}}(w)\right)^{2}\big(K(v,w)+K(w,v)\big)\,{\rm d}w

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{B_{\rho}(v)\setminus B_{3\rho}(w_{0})}\left(H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{0})-H^{\frac{1}{2}}(w)\right)^{2}K(v_{0},w)\,{\rm d}w

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{B_{\rho}(v)\setminus B_{3\rho}(w_{0})}\left|\nabla_{v}H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{0})\right|^{2}\left|v_{0}-w\right|^{2}K(v_{0},w)\,{\rm d}w

\displaystyle\leq C\rho^{2-2s}\frac{1}{36\rho^{2}}\left(\log\Big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta}\Big)\right)^{2}e^{-\frac{1}{3\rho}\left|v_{0}-w_{0}\right|\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta}\big)}

\displaystyle=C\rho^{-2s}\left(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta}\right)\frac{k\delta}{\rho^{2s}}=:c_{1}\rho^{-2s},

for some constant
c_{1}>0
independent of
\rho,\delta(t),k
.
Together with

\mathcal{T}H(t,x,v)=-\frac{k}{\rho^{2s}},

we thus find

\mathcal{T}H+\mathcal{L}_{\rho}H^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq 0

for
k\geq c_{1}
.

iv.

For
(t,x,v)
such that
2\rho<\left|v-w_{0}\right|<3\rho
and
\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}>3\rho
.
Then

H(t,x,v)=e^{-\frac{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}}{3\rho}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)},

but for
w\in B_{\rho}(v)
it might happen that
\left|w-w_{0}\right|>\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}>3\rho
, in which case

H(t,x,w)=e^{-\frac{1}{3\rho}\left|w-w_{0}\right|\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)}\leq e^{-\frac{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}}{3\rho}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)}=H(t,x,v).

Similar to before, we fix
v_{0}\in B_{\rho}(v)
with
\left|v_{0}-w_{0}\right|=\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}
such that for every
w\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{(1+2s)}}}(w_{0})
it holds
\left|v_{0}-w\right|\leq 2\left|v-w\right|
. For instance, we choose
v_{0}\in\partial B_{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{(1+2s)}}}(w_{0})
so that
v_{0}
minimises the distance between
v
and

{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{(1+2s)}}}(w_{0})
.
Then
B_{\rho}(v)\subset B_{2\rho}(v_{0})
and
H(t,x,v_{0})=H(t,x,v)
. If we denote by
\Omega:=B_{\rho}(v)\setminus B_{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{(1+2s)}}}(w_{0})
, then we find similar to before

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\rho}H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{\rho}(v)}\left(H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)-H^{\frac{1}{2}}(w)\right)^{2}\big(K(v,w)+K(w,v)\big)\,{\rm d}w

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\left(H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)-H^{\frac{1}{2}}(w)\right)^{2}\big(K(v,w)+K(w,v)\big)\,{\rm d}w

\displaystyle\leq\left|\nabla_{v}H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{0})\right|^{2}\int_{\Omega}\left|v_{0}-w\right|^{2}K(v_{0},w)\,{\rm d}w

\displaystyle\leq C\rho^{2-2s}\frac{1}{36\rho^{2}}\left(\log\Big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta}\Big)\right)^{2}e^{-\frac{1}{3\rho}\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta}\big)}

\displaystyle=:c_{2}\frac{1}{k\delta(t)}e^{-\frac{1}{3\rho}\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta}\big)},

for
c_{2}
independent of
k,\rho,\delta(t)
.
Finally, using
\log a<a
for
a>0

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}H(t,x,v)

\displaystyle=-\Bigg[\frac{1}{3\rho(1+2s)}\log\Big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\Big)\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{-\frac{1+4s}{1+2s}}(X-Y_{0})\cdot(v-w_{0})

\displaystyle+\frac{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}}{3\rho\delta(t)}\Bigg]H(t,x,v)

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg[\frac{1}{3\rho}\log\Big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\Big)\frac{\rho}{\rho^{2s}}-\frac{1}{\delta(t)}\Bigg]e^{-\frac{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}}{3\rho}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)}

\displaystyle=:c_{3}\left[\frac{1}{\delta(t)k}-\frac{1}{\delta(t)}\right]e^{-\frac{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}}{3\rho}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)}.

Thus
\mathcal{T}H+\mathcal{L}_{\rho}H^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq 0
, for
k\geq c_{2}+c_{3}
.

v.

For
(t,x,v)
such that
3\rho<\left|v-w_{0}\right|
and either
\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}<3\rho
or

3\rho<\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}<\left|v-w_{0}\right|
:
Then

H(t,x,v):=e^{-\frac{\left|v-w_{0}\right|}{3\rho}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)},

and

\mathcal{T}H(t,x,v)=-\frac{\left|v-w_{0}\right|}{3\rho\delta(t)}e^{-\frac{\left|v-w_{0}\right|}{3\rho}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)}\leq-\frac{c_{4}}{\delta(t)}e^{-\frac{\left|v-w_{0}\right|}{3\rho}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)},

for some
c_{4}>0
independent of
k,\delta(t),\rho
.
Moreover, again as before, using the fact that
\left|w-w_{0}\right|>\left|v-w_{0}\right|-\rho
we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\rho}H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{\rho}(v)}\left(H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)-H^{\frac{1}{2}}(w)\right)^{2}\big(K(v,w)+K(w,v)\big)\,{\rm d}w

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{v^{\prime}\in B_{\rho}(v)}\left|\nabla_{v}H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v^{\prime})\right|^{2}\int_{B_{\rho}(v)}\left|v-w\right|^{2}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w

\displaystyle\leq C\rho^{2-2s}e^{-\frac{\left|v-w_{0}\right|-\rho}{3\rho}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)}\Bigg[\log\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\Bigg)\Bigg]^{2}\frac{1}{(6\rho)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\rho^{2-2s}e^{-\frac{\left|v-w_{0}\right|}{3\rho}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)}\Bigg[\log\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\Bigg)\Bigg]^{2}\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{3}}\frac{1}{(6\rho)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{5}}{k\delta(t)}e^{-\frac{\left|v-w_{0}\right|}{3\rho}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)},

for some
c_{5}>0
independent of
k,\rho,\delta(t)
.
Thus

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}H+\mathcal{L}_{\rho}H^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\left[-\frac{c_{4}}{\delta(t)}+\frac{c_{5}}{k\delta(t)}\right]e^{-\frac{\left|v-w_{0}\right|}{3\rho}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)}\leq 0,

for
k>\frac{c_{5}}{c_{4}}
.

vi.

For
(t,x,v)
such that
3\rho<\left|v-w_{0}\right|<\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}
.
Then

H(t,x,v):=e^{-\frac{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}}{3\rho}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)},

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}H(t,x,v)

\displaystyle=-\Bigg[\frac{1}{3\rho(1+2s)}\log\Big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\Big)\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{-\frac{1+4s}{1+2s}}(X-Y_{0})\cdot(v-w_{0})

\displaystyle+\frac{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}}{3\rho\delta(t)}\Bigg]H(t,x,v)

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}}{\rho\delta(t)}\Bigg[\frac{\rho^{2s}\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{-2s}{1+2s}}}{k}-1\Bigg]H(t,x,v)

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}}{\rho\delta(t)}\Bigg[\frac{1}{k}-1\Bigg]e^{-\frac{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}}{3\rho}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)}

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg[\frac{1}{\delta(t)k}-\frac{1}{\delta(t)}\Bigg]e^{-\frac{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}}{3\rho}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)}

\displaystyle=:c_{6}\Bigg[\frac{1}{\delta(t)k}-\frac{1}{\delta(t)}\Bigg]e^{-\frac{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}}{3\rho}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)}.

The last inequality uses
k>1
.
Moreover, using
\log a<a^{1/2}
we denote by

\Omega:=B_{\rho}(v)\setminus B_{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{1/(1+2s)}}(w_{0}),

and we again take
v_{0}\in\partial B_{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{1/(1+2s)}}(w_{0})
,
so that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\rho}H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\left(H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)-H^{\frac{1}{2}}(w)\right)^{2}\big(K(v,w)+K(w,v)\big)\,{\rm d}w

\displaystyle\leq C\rho^{2-2s}\left|\nabla_{v}H^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{0})\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\rho^{2-2s}\left[\log\Big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\Big)\right]^{2}\Big(\frac{1}{6\rho}\Big)^{2}e^{-\frac{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}}{3\rho}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{k\delta(t)}e^{-\frac{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}}{3\rho}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)}

\displaystyle=:c_{7}\frac{1}{k\delta(t)}e^{-\frac{\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}}{3\rho}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)}.

Thus, for (
77
) to hold in this case, we need
k>\frac{c_{7}}{c_{6}}
.

Overall, we conclude the proof upon choosing

k>\max\left\{1,c_{1},c_{2}+c_{3},\frac{c_{5}}{c_{4}},\frac{c_{7}}{c_{6}}\right\},

where
c_{i}>0
for
i=1,\dots,7
are constants independent of
\rho
and
\delta(t)
.
∎

6.3.3.
Proof of Aronson’s bound

The existence of a function satisfying (
77
) given by Lemma
18
combined with Proposition
17
implies Theorem
16
.

Proof of Theorem
16
.

Step 1: Bounds on
H
.

We recall the definition of
H
from (
89
): for
\delta(t)=2(\sigma-\tau_{0})-(t-\tau_{0})
we have defined

H(t,x,v)=e^{-\max\Big\{1,\frac{1}{3\rho}\max\Big(\left|v-w_{0}\right|,\left|x-y_{0}-(\sigma+t-2\tau_{0})w_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}\Big)\Big\}\log\big(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\big)}.

Step 1-(i): The lower bound.

For
(t,x,v)\in[\tau_{0},\sigma]\times B_{(2\rho)^{1+2s}}(y_{0}+(\sigma+t-2\tau_{0})w_{0})\times B_{2\rho}(w_{0})
we find

(91)

H(t,x,v)\geq\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\Bigg)^{-1}\geq\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{-1}.

We used
\delta(t)\geq\sigma-\tau_{0}
and
\sigma-\tau_{0}\leq\frac{\rho^{2s}}{4k}
.

Step 1-(ii): The upper bound.

For
t=\tau_{0}
and
(x,v)
such that

\max\big\{\left|x-y_{0}-(\sigma-\tau_{0})w_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}},\left|v-w_{0}\right|\big\}\geq\gamma

we find

(92)

H(\tau_{0},x,v)\leq\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{-\frac{\gamma}{3\rho}}\leq\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{2k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{-\frac{\gamma}{3\rho}}.

We used
\sigma-\tau_{0}\leq\frac{\rho^{2s}}{4k}
.

Step 1-(iii): The general upper bound.

Note that for
(t,x,v)\in[\tau_{0},\sigma]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
we have

(93)

H(t,x,v)\leq\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k\delta(t)}\Bigg)^{-1}\leq\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{2k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{-1}.

Step 2: Assembly.

For fixed
(y_{0},w_{0})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
we denote

Q^{0}_{2\rho}(t,y_{0},w_{0}):=B_{(2\rho)^{1+2s}}(y_{0}+(\sigma+t-2\tau_{0})w_{0})\times B_{2\rho}(w_{0}),

to obtain with Proposition
17
, (
92
) and (
93
)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(\tau_{0},\sigma)}

\displaystyle\int_{Q^{0}_{2\rho}(t,y_{0},w_{0})}f^{2}(t,x,v)H(t,x,v)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in(\tau_{0},\sigma)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f^{2}(t,x,v)H(t,x,v)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}\setminus Q^{0}_{\gamma}(\tau_{0},y_{0},w_{0})}f^{2}(\tau_{0},x,v)H(\tau_{0},x,v)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+C\rho^{-2s}\left\|H\right\|_{L^{\infty}([\tau_{0},\sigma]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}([\tau_{0},\sigma]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{2k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{-\frac{\gamma}{3\rho}}\left\|f_{0}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}+C\rho^{-2s}\left(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{2k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\right)^{-1}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}([\tau_{0},\sigma]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}^{2}.

This implies due to (
91
)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(\tau_{0},\sigma)}\int_{Q^{0}_{2\rho}(t,y_{0},w_{0})}f^{2}(t,x,v)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{2k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{-\frac{\gamma}{3\rho}}\left\|f_{0}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}

\displaystyle+C\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)\left(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{2k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\right)^{-1}\rho^{-2s}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}([\tau_{0},\sigma]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{1-\frac{\gamma}{3\rho}}\left\|f_{0}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}+C\rho^{-2s}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}([\tau_{0},\sigma]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}^{2}.

Since we assume that (
1
) admits a fundamental solution, we estimate with Young’s convolution inequality and with (
4
)

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}([\tau_{0},\sigma]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|f_{0}\ast_{v}J\right\|_{L^{2}([\tau_{0},\sigma]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}^{2}\leq(\sigma-\tau_{0})\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}^{2}\left\|J\right\|_{L^{\infty}([\tau_{0},\sigma];L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d}))}^{2}

\displaystyle=(\sigma-\tau_{0})\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}^{2},

so that

(94)

\sup_{t\in(\tau_{0},\sigma)}\int_{Q^{0}_{2\rho}(t,y_{0},w_{0})}f^{2}(t,x,v)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v\leq\Bigg[\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{1-\frac{\gamma}{3\rho}}+\rho^{-2s}(\sigma-\tau_{0})\Bigg]\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}^{2}.

Then using Proposition
5
with
r^{2s}=\frac{\sigma-\tau_{0}}{2^{2s}}
,
R=2r
for
\rho\geq r
and (
94
)

\displaystyle\left|f(\sigma,y_{0},w_{0})\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{Q_{r}(\sigma,y_{0},w_{0})}f^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{-(2d(1+s)+2s)}\int_{Q_{2r}(\sigma,y_{0},w_{0})}f^{2}\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{-(2d(1+s)+2s)+2s}\sup_{t\in(\tau_{0},\sigma)}\int_{Q^{0}_{2\rho}(t,y_{0},w_{0})}f^{2}(t,x,v)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq C(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{2d(1+s)}{2s}}\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{1-\frac{\gamma}{3\rho}}\left\|f_{0}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}

\displaystyle+\rho^{-2s}(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{1-\frac{2d(1+s)}{2s}}\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}^{2}.

This concludes the proof of Theorem
16
.
∎

6.4.
Proof of the polynomial decay

With Theorem
14
and Theorem
15
at hand, we use standard methods to derive the decay on the fundamental solution.

Proof of Theorem
2
.

Due to the on-diagonal bounds (
71
), it suffices to consider
(\sigma-\tau_{0})\leq\frac{\rho^{2s}}{4k}
.
We choose

(95)

\rho=\frac{\gamma}{12}\left(\frac{1}{4}+\frac{2d(1+s)+2s}{2s}\right)^{-1}.

If we abbreviate
\left|X-Y_{0}\right|:=\left|x-y_{0}-(\sigma-\tau_{0})(v-w_{0})\right|
, then the off-diagonal bounds from Theorem
15
yield

\displaystyle J(\sigma,x,v;\tau_{0},y_{0},w_{0})\lesssim

\displaystyle(\sigma-\tau_{0})\Big(\max\left\{\left|v-w_{0}\right|,\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}\right\}\Big)^{-(2d(1+s)+2s)}

\displaystyle+(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{2d(1+s)}{2s}}\left(\max\left\{\left|v-w_{0}\right|,\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}\right\}\right)^{-\frac{s}{2}},

which concludes the proof of Theorem
2
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4
.

Again, due to the on-diagonal bounds (
71
), it suffices to consider
(\sigma-\tau_{0})\leq\frac{\rho^{2s}}{4k}
. Due to (
11
), it suffices to use Aronson’s method on
J_{\rho}
. In particular, the term arising from the non-singular part in Proposition
17
vanishes, that is
\mathcal{I}_{loc}^{ns}=0
in (
82
). This implies that in Theorem
16
only the first term on the right hand side remains, from which we deduce Theorem
15
on
J_{\rho}
with an improved right hand side:

J_{\rho}(\sigma,x,v;\tau_{0},y_{0},w_{0})\leq C(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{2d(1+s)}{2s}}\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\gamma}{12\rho}}.

If we choose
\rho
as above in (
95
) then together with (
11
) this yields for
(\sigma-\tau_{0})\leq\frac{\rho^{2s}}{4k}

\displaystyle J(\sigma,x,v;\tau_{0},y_{0},w_{0})\lesssim(\sigma-\tau_{0})\max\left\{\left|v-w_{0}\right|,\left|X-Y_{0}\right|^{\frac{1}{1+2s}}\right\}\Big)^{-(2d(1+s)+2s)},

which concludes the proof of Theorem
4
.
∎

7.
Lower bound on the fundamental solution

7.1.
Pointwise Harnack inequality

Theorem 19
.

Let
s\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
, and let
f
be a non-negative solution of
(
1
) in
[0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
, with zero source term and
with a non-negative kernel satisfying
(
15
)–(
18
).
Let
0<\tau_{0}<t\leq T,(y_{0},w_{0}),(x,v)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}.

Then there exists a constant
C
depending on
s,d,\lambda_{0},\Lambda_{0}
and
T
such that

(96)

\displaystyle f(\tau_{0},y_{0},w_{0})\leq{}

\displaystyle f(t,x,v)\exp\Bigg[C\Bigg(1+\frac{t-\tau_{0}}{\tau_{0}}+\left(\frac{\left|v-w_{0}\right|}{(t-\tau_{0})^{\frac{1}{2s}}}+\frac{\left|x-y_{0}-(t-\tau_{0})w_{0}\right|}{(t-\tau_{0})^{1+\frac{1}{2s}}}\right)^{\frac{2s}{2s-1}}\Bigg)\Bigg].

Proof.

By scaling and translating Theorem
1
, there
exist constants

c_{\rm H}\in(0,1),\qquad C_{\rm H}>1,

depending only on
s,d,\lambda_{0},\Lambda_{0}
, with the following
property. Let
\delta>0
, and suppose that the kinetic cylinder
required below is contained in the domain of the equation. If

\left|v_{2}-v_{1}\right|\leq c_{\rm H}\delta^{\frac{1}{2s}}

and

\left|x_{2}-x_{1}-\delta v_{1}\right|\leq c_{\rm H}\delta^{1+\frac{1}{2s}},

then

(97)

f(t_{1},x_{1},v_{1})\leq C_{\rm H}f(t_{1}+\delta,x_{2},v_{2}).

Let

D:=x-y_{0}-(t-\tau_{0})w_{0},\qquad B:=\frac{D}{t-\tau_{0}}-\frac{(v-w_{0})}{2}.

For
a\in[0,1]
, set

V(a):=w_{0}+a(v-w_{0})+6a(1-a)B

and

X(a):=y_{0}+(t-\tau_{0})\int_{0}^{a}V(b)\,\,{\rm d}b.

Then

V(0)=w_{0},\qquad V(1)=v,\qquad X(0)=y_{0},\qquad X(1)=x,

because

\int_{0}^{1}V(a)\,\,{\rm d}a=w_{0}+\frac{(v-w_{0})}{2}+B=\frac{x-y_{0}}{(t-\tau_{0})}.

Moreover,

X^{\prime}(a)=(t-\tau_{0})V(a)

and

\left\|V^{\prime}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,1)}\leq C\left(\left|(v-w_{0})\right|+\frac{\left|D\right|}{(t-\tau_{0})}\right).

For
\nu=0,\dots,N
, define

t^{\nu}:=\tau_{0}+\frac{\nu}{N}(t-\tau_{0}),\qquad x^{\nu}:=X\left(\frac{\nu}{N}\right),\qquad v^{\nu}:=V\left(\frac{\nu}{N}\right).

Thus

(t^{0},x^{0},v^{0})=(\tau_{0},y_{0},w_{0}),\qquad(t^{N},x^{N},v^{N})=(t,x,v).

Set

\mathcal{D}:=\frac{\left|v-w_{0}\right|}{(t-\tau_{0})^{1/(2s)}}+\frac{\left|x-y_{0}-(t-\tau_{0})w_{0}\right|}{(t-\tau_{0})^{1+1/(2s)}}.

For every
\nu=0,\dots,N-1
, the mean-value theorem gives

\left|v^{\nu+1}-v^{\nu}\right|\leq C(t-\tau_{0})^{1/(2s)}\frac{\mathcal{D}}{N}.

Furthermore, since
X^{\prime}=(t-\tau_{0})V
,

\displaystyle x^{\nu+1}-x^{\nu}-(t^{\nu+1}-t^{\nu})v^{\nu}=(t-\tau_{0})\int_{\nu/N}^{(\nu+1)/N}\left[V(a)-V\left(\frac{\nu}{N}\right)\right]\,{\rm d}a,

and hence

\left|x^{\nu+1}-x^{\nu}-(t^{\nu+1}-t^{\nu})v^{\nu}\right|\leq C(t-\tau_{0})^{1+1/(2s)}\frac{\mathcal{D}}{N^{2}}.

We choose

N=\left\lceil C_{0}\left(1+\frac{t-\tau_{0}}{\tau_{0}}+\mathcal{D}^{\frac{2s}{2s-1}}\right)\right\rceil,

where
C_{0}
is sufficiently large depending on the one-link Harnack geometry.
Since
s>\frac{1}{2}
, choosing
C_{0}
sufficiently large ensures

\left|v^{\nu+1}-v^{\nu}\right|\leq c_{\rm H}\left(\frac{t-\tau_{0}}{N}\right)^{1/(2s)}

and

\left|x^{\nu+1}-x^{\nu}-(t^{\nu+1}-t^{\nu})v^{\nu}\right|\leq c_{\rm H}\left(\frac{(t-\tau_{0})}{N}\right)^{\frac{1+2s}{2s}}.

Indeed, the choice of
N
gives

\mathcal{D}\leq CN^{\frac{2s-1}{2s}}.

Therefore

\frac{\mathcal{D}}{N}\leq CN^{-\frac{1}{2s}},\qquad\frac{\mathcal{D}}{N^{2}}\leq CN^{-1-\frac{1}{2s}}.

Moreover,

N\geq C_{0}\frac{t-\tau_{0}}{\tau_{0}},

and hence

\frac{t-\tau_{0}}{N}\leq\frac{\tau_{0}}{C_{0}}.

Thus, by choosing
C_{0}
sufficiently large, all the kinetic
cylinders used in the chain are contained in

[0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
.

The one-link inequality (
97
) therefore gives

f(t^{\nu},x^{\nu},v^{\nu})\leq C_{\rm H}f(t^{\nu+1},x^{\nu+1},v^{\nu+1})

for every
\nu=0,\dots,N-1
. Iterating,

f(\tau_{0},y_{0},w_{0})\leq C_{\rm H}^{N}f(t,x,v).

Finally,

C_{\rm H}^{N}=\exp(N\log C_{\rm H})

and, by the definition of
N
,

N\leq C\left(1+\frac{t-\tau_{0}}{\tau_{0}}+\mathcal{D}^{\frac{2s}{2s-1}}\right).

Thus

\displaystyle f(\tau_{0},y_{0},w_{0})\leq{}

\displaystyle f(t,x,v)\exp\Bigg[C\Bigg(1+\frac{t-\tau_{0}}{\tau_{0}}+\left(\frac{\left|v-w_{0}\right|}{(t-\tau_{0})^{\frac{1}{2s}}}+\frac{\left|x-y_{0}-(t-\tau_{0})w_{0}\right|}{(t-\tau_{0})^{1+\frac{1}{2s}}}\right)^{\frac{2s}{2s-1}}\Bigg)\Bigg].

∎

7.2.
Sufficient condition for the exponential lower bound

Theorem 20
.

Let
s\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
.
Let
f
be a non-negative weak solution of (
1
) in
[0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
with (
15
)-(
18
) that is essentially bounded almost everywhere. Suppose that for some
\alpha>0
there holds

(98)

\mathcal{M}:=\inf_{0<t<T}\int_{\max\left\{\left|x\right|^{\frac{2s}{1+2s}},\left|v\right|^{2s}\right\}<\alpha t}f(t,x,v)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v>0.

Then there exists constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that

f(t,x,v)\geq C_{1}t^{-\frac{d(1+s)}{s}}\exp\left[-C_{2}\left(1+\left(\frac{|v|}{t^{1/(2s)}}+\frac{|x|}{t^{1+1/(2s)}}\right)^{\frac{2s}{2s-1}}\right)\right].

The constant
C_{1}
depends on
s,d,\alpha,T,\lambda_{0},\Lambda_{0}
and
\mathcal{M}
, whereas
C_{2}
depends only on
s,d,T,\lambda_{0},\Lambda_{0}
.

Remark 5
.

To draw a connection to the literature on hypoelliptic lower bounds, there has been a result discussing Gaussian lower bounds for solutions to the Boltzmann equation by Imbert-Mouhot-Silvestre in
[
20
]
. The authors show that for any
t\in[0,T]
there exists
a(t),b(t)>0
such that any non-negative solution of the Boltzmann equation satisfies

f(t,x,v)\geq a(t)e^{-b(t)\left|v\right|^{2}}.

We explicitly give expressions for
a
and
b
, however, we are only able to treat symmetric kernels due to the tail bound of Section
3
.

Proof.

Consider
y,w\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
such that

\max\left\{\left|y\right|^{\frac{2s}{1+2s}},\left|w\right|^{2s}\right\}<\frac{\alpha t}{4}.

By Theorem
19
, there exists

C_{\alpha}>0
, depending only on

s,d,\lambda_{0},\Lambda_{0},T
and
\alpha
, such that

(99)

f(t/4,y,w)\leq C_{\alpha}f(t/2,0,0).

Moreover,

\left|\left\{(y,w)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}:\max\left\{\left|y\right|^{\frac{2s}{1+2s}},\left|w\right|^{2s}\right\}<\frac{\alpha t}{4}\right\}\right|\approx t^{\frac{2d(1+s)}{2s}},

where the implicit constants may depend on
\alpha,d,s
.
Integrating (
99
) over this set and using
(
98
) at time
t/4
, we obtain

\displaystyle f(t/2,0,0)

\displaystyle\geq Ct^{-\frac{2d(1+s)}{2s}}C_{\alpha}^{-1}\int_{\max\left\{\left|y\right|^{\frac{2s}{1+2s}},\left|w\right|^{2s}\right\}<\frac{\alpha t}{4}}f(t/4,y,w)\,\,{\rm d}y\,\,{\rm d}w

\displaystyle\geq C\mathcal{M}t^{-\frac{2d(1+s)}{2s}}C_{\alpha}^{-1}.

On the other hand, Theorem
19
, applied between

(t/2,0,0)
and
(t,x,v)
, gives

f(t/2,0,0)\leq f(t,x,v)\exp\left[C\left(1+\left(\frac{|v|}{t^{1/(2s)}}+\frac{|x|}{t^{1+1/(2s)}}\right)^{\frac{2s}{2s-1}}\right)\right].

Combining the preceding two estimates, we obtain

f(t,x,v)\geq c\,\mathcal{M}\,t^{-\frac{d(1+s)}{s}}\exp\left[-C\left(\frac{|v|}{t^{1/(2s)}}+\frac{|x|}{t^{1+1/(2s)}}\right)^{\frac{2s}{2s-1}}\right],

after absorbing all constants depending on
\alpha
into
c
.
This proves the theorem.
∎

7.3.
Proof of Theorem
3

Proof of Theorem
3
.

We fix
0<\tau_{0}<\sigma<T
. We first establish a uniform lower bound on the mass of the
fundamental solution in a kinetic ball centred on the characteristic
starting from
(y_{0},w_{0})
.

Step 1: Uniform near-diagonal mass bound.

For
0<t<T-\tau_{0}
, we define

A_{t}:=\left\{(x,v)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}:\max\left\{\left|x-y_{0}-tw_{0}\right|^{\frac{2s}{1+2s}},\left|v-w_{0}\right|^{2s}\right\}<\alpha t\right\},

where
\alpha>0
will be chosen below. For

\tau_{0}\leq\tau<\tau_{0}+t
, we let

F_{t}(\tau,y,w):=\int_{A_{t}}J(\tau_{0}+t,x,v;\tau,y,w)\,\,{\rm d}x\,\,{\rm d}v.

By the non-negativity and normalisation of the fundamental solution,
0\leq F_{t}\leq 1
.
Moreover, as a function of the initial variables

(\tau,y,w)
,
F_{t}
solves the backward equation

\partial_{\tau}F_{t}+w\cdot\nabla_{y}F_{t}+\mathcal{L}_{\tau}F_{t}=0

in
(\tau_{0},\tau_{0}+t)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
, and its terminal value is

F_{t}(\tau_{0}+t,y,w)=\mathbbm{1}_{A_{t}}(y,w).

We reverse time and velocity by setting

G_{t}(r,y,\omega):=F_{t}(\tau_{0}+t-r,y,-\omega),\qquad 0<r\leq t.

Then
G_{t}
solves a forward kinetic equation

\partial_{r}G_{t}+\omega\cdot\nabla_{y}G_{t}=\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{r}G_{t},

where the transformed kernel is obtained from
K
by time reversal
and velocity reflection. In particular, it satisfies the same
coercivity, integral upper-bound and symmetry assumptions, with the
same structural constants. The initial value of
G_{t}
is

G_{t}(0,y,\omega)=\mathbbm{1}_{A_{t}}(y,-\omega).

This function equals one in a kinetic neighbourhood of

(y_{0}+tw_{0},-w_{0}).

The characteristic for the reversed equation starting
from this point reaches
(y_{0},-w_{0})
at time
t
, since

y_{0}+tw_{0}+t(-w_{0})=y_{0}.

We then choose a universal
c_{0}>0
sufficiently small and

\alpha>0
sufficiently large that the kinetic cylinder of radius

r_{t}:=c_{0}t^{\frac{1}{2s}}

used below has its section at time
r=0
compactly contained in

\left\{(y,\omega):(y,-\omega)\in A_{t}\right\}.

We extend
G_{t}
locally to negative times by setting it equal to one
there. Since its trace at
r=0
is also equal to one on the
spatial-velocity section of this cylinder, the extension introduces
no jump in the cylinder. It is therefore a non-negative
super-solution there.

After translating the cylinder and rescaling it by
r_{t}
to a
fixed unit kinetic cylinder, the past cylinder lies in the region
where the extended function is identically one, while the
corresponding future cylinder contains

(t,y_{0},-w_{0})
. The scaled Weak Harnack inequality,
Theorem
9
, therefore gives

1\leq C\inf_{\widetilde{Q}^{+}}G_{t}\leq CG_{t}(t,y_{0},-w_{0}),

where
C
depends only on

d,s,\lambda_{0},\Lambda_{0}
and the fixed cylinder geometry.
Consequently,

F_{t}(\tau_{0},y_{0},w_{0})=G_{t}(t,y_{0},-w_{0})\geq c

for a constant
c>0
independent of
t
. Hence

(100)

\inf_{0<t<T-\tau_{0}}\int_{A_{t}}J(\tau_{0}+t,x,v;\tau_{0},y_{0},w_{0})\,\,{\rm d}x\,\,{\rm d}v\geq c>0.

Step 2: Reduction to unit time.

For
0<\theta<T_{*}:=\frac{T-\tau_{0}}{\sigma-\tau_{0}},

we define the rescaled fundamental solution

\displaystyle U(\theta,X,V):={}

\displaystyle(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{\frac{2d(1+s)}{2s}}J\Big(\tau_{0}+(\sigma-\tau_{0})\theta,\,y_{0}+(\sigma-\tau_{0})\theta w_{0}+(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{\frac{1+2s}{2s}}X,\,w_{0}+(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{\frac{1}{2s}}V;\,\tau_{0},y_{0},w_{0}\Big).

By the scaling and Galilean invariance of the equation,
U
is a
non-negative solution of an equation of the form (
1
)
whose kernel satisfies the same structural assumptions.

For every
0<\theta<1
, the change of variables

x=y_{0}+(\sigma-\tau_{0})\theta w_{0}+(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{\frac{1+2s}{2s}}X,\qquad v=w_{0}+(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{\frac{1}{2s}}V

and (
100
) give

\displaystyle\int_{\max\left\{\left|X\right|^{\frac{2s}{1+2s}},\left|V\right|^{2s}\right\}<\alpha\theta}U(\theta,X,V)\,\,{\rm d}X\,\,{\rm d}V

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{\max\left\{\left|x-y_{0}-(\sigma-\tau_{0})\theta w_{0}\right|^{\frac{2s}{1+2s}},\left|v-w_{0}\right|^{2s}\right\}<\alpha(\sigma-\tau_{0})\theta}J(\tau_{0}+(\sigma-\tau_{0})\theta,x,v;\tau_{0},y_{0},w_{0})\,\,{\rm d}x\,\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\qquad\geq c.

Therefore

\inf_{0<\theta<1}\int_{\max\left\{\left|X\right|^{\frac{2s}{1+2s}},\left|V\right|^{2s}\right\}<\alpha\theta}U(\theta,X,V)\,\,{\rm d}X\,\,{\rm d}V\geq c>0.

The Nash upper bound, Theorem
14
, shows that
U
is
locally essentially bounded for every positive time. Thus the proof
of Theorem
20
, which only uses positive times,
applies to
U
. We consequently obtain

U(1,X,V)\geq c\exp\left[-C\left(|V|+|X|\right)^{\frac{2s}{2s-1}}\right].

Step 3: Return to the original variables.

Taking

X=\frac{x-y_{0}-(\sigma-\tau_{0})w_{0}}{(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{\frac{1+2s}{2s}}},\qquad V=\frac{v-w_{0}}{(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{\frac{1}{2s}}},

we conclude that

\displaystyle J(\sigma,x,v;\tau_{0},y_{0},w_{0})\geq{}

\displaystyle c(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{d(1+s)}{s}}

\displaystyle\times\exp\left[-C\left(\frac{|v-w_{0}|}{(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{1/(2s)}}+\frac{|x-y_{0}-(\sigma-\tau_{0})w_{0}|}{(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{1+1/(2s)}}\right)^{\frac{2s}{2s-1}}\right],

which proves Theorem
3
.
∎
