Appendix A
Higher regularity estimate

The purpose of this appendix is to clarify the use of a test function with less differentiability than what is usually required. This is necessary to justify the construction of the test function in the argument of Proposition
11
in case that
s\geq\frac{1}{2}
.

One can show that solutions to the fractional Laplacian of order
2s\in(0,2)
are in fact
H^{2s-}
, rather than
H^{s}
as expected from an energy estimate. Therefore the equation can in principle be tested against a function in
H^{0+}
. The test function we work with in Proposition
11
is in
H^{\frac{s}{2}}
for any
s\in(0,1)
; thus, if we can show that the solution to (
1
) satisfies better regularity, say
H^{\frac{3s}{2}}
, then this test function is indeed permissible.

To this end, we compare our solutions to the solution of the fractional Laplacian, so that we can quantify the loss of regularity from the roughening of the coefficient. We show that we can work with a solution that satisfies the higher differentiability that is required in order for the test function in (
32
) to be admissible. In the limit, where the smoothening of the fractional Laplacian is lost, this higher regularity vanishes as well. However, we can run the argument of Proposition
11
on the solutions before we take the limit, where the admissibility of the test function is guaranteed. Since the higher regularity is not used quantitatively, but merely qualitatively, we end up with the result of Proposition
11
uniformly in the approximation parameter. We can then take the limit in the end, so that the results apply to solutions of (
1
). The equation is linear, so that the solution is unique, and the limit is uniquely determined through the limit equation, which corresponds to the equation with rough coefficients.

Let
\eta:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
be a cutoff, such that
0\leq\eta\leq 1
with
\eta=1
in
B_{1}
,
\eta=0
outside
B_{2}
and
\eta(v)=-\left|v\right|+2
for
v\in B_{2}\setminus B_{1}
. Denote by
\eta_{\delta}
the cutoff
\eta_{\delta}(v)=\eta\big(\frac{v}{\delta}\big)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(B_{2\delta})
satisfying
0\leq\eta_{\delta}\leq 1
with
\eta_{\delta}=1
in
B_{\delta}
and
\eta_{\delta}=0
outside
B_{2\delta}
. Then we consider

K_{\delta}(v,w)=\eta_{\delta}(v-w)c_{d,s}\left|v-w\right|^{-(d+2s)}+\big(1-\eta_{\delta}(v-w)\big)K(v,w),

where
c_{d}>0
is the constant such that

(-\Delta_{v})^{s}f(v)=c_{d,s}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big(f(w)-f(v)\big)\left|v-w\right|^{-(d+2s)}\,{\rm d}w.

Note that
\eta_{\delta}\xrightarrow[\delta\to 0]{}0,

pointwise almost everywhere and in
L^{p}
for any
1\leq p<\infty
.
Now let
f_{\delta}
solve

(101)

\mathcal{T}f_{\delta}=\mathcal{L}_{\delta}f_{\delta}:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big(f_{\delta}(w)-f_{\delta}(v)\big)K_{\delta}(v,w)\,{\rm d}w,

in
[t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.
Note

f_{\delta}\in L^{2}((t_{1},t_{2})\times B_{R^{1+2s}};H^{s}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))

uniformly in
\delta
. To see this, we test (
101
) with
f_{\delta}\phi^{2}
where
\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}([t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}})
is a cutoff in time and space such that
\phi=1
in
\big[\frac{t_{1}}{2},\frac{t_{2}}{2}\big]\times B_{\big(\frac{R}{2}\big)^{1+2s}}
and
\phi=0
outside
[t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}
. We get

\displaystyle\int_{[t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\mathcal{T}f_{\delta}f_{\delta}\phi^{2}\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle=\int_{[t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big(f_{\delta}(w)-f_{\delta}(v)\big)f_{\delta}(v)\phi^{2}(t,x)K_{\delta}(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{[t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}}\phi^{2}(t,x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big(f_{\delta}(w)-f_{\delta}(v)\big)^{2}K_{\delta}(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t.

\displaystyle=-\frac{c_{d}}{2}\int_{[t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}}\phi^{2}(t,x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\eta_{\delta}(v-w)\frac{\big(f_{\delta}(w)-f_{\delta}(v)\big)^{2}}{\left|v-w\right|^{d+2s}}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{[t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}}\phi^{2}(t,x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}(1-\eta_{\delta}(v-w))\big(f_{\delta}(w)-f_{\delta}(v)\big)^{2}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z.

Using that
(1-\eta_{\delta})\geq\frac{1}{4}
outside
B_{\frac{5\delta}{4}}
and
\eta_{\delta}\geq\frac{1}{4}
inside
B_{\frac{7\delta}{4}}
, we find with the coercivity of
K
(
15
)

\displaystyle\int_{[t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\mathcal{T}f_{\delta}f_{\delta}\phi^{2}\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{4}\frac{c_{d}}{2}\int_{[t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}}\phi^{2}(t,x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{\left|v-w\right|<\frac{7\delta}{4}}\frac{\big(f_{\delta}(w)-f_{\delta}(v)\big)^{2}}{\left|v-w\right|^{d+2s}}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\cdot\frac{1}{2}\int_{[t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}}\phi^{2}(t,x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{\left|v-w\right|>\frac{5\delta}{4}}\big(f_{\delta}(w)-f_{\delta}(v)\big)^{2}K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{4}\frac{c_{d}}{2}\int_{[t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}}\phi^{2}(t,x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{\left|v-w\right|<\frac{7\delta}{4}}\frac{\big(f_{\delta}(w)-f_{\delta}(v)\big)^{2}}{\left|v-w\right|^{d+2s}}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\frac{\lambda}{2}\int_{[t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}}\phi^{2}(t,x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{\left|v-w\right|>\frac{5\delta}{4}}\frac{\big(f_{\delta}(w)-f_{\delta}(v)\big)^{2}}{\left|v-w\right|^{d+2s}}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z.

Thus

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]}

\displaystyle\int_{B_{(\frac{R}{2})^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}f_{\delta}^{2}(t,x,v)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+C_{d,s,\lambda}\int_{\big[\frac{t_{1}}{2},\frac{t_{2}}{2}\big]\times B_{(\frac{R}{2})^{1+2s}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\frac{\big(f_{\delta}(w)-f_{\delta}(v)\big)^{2}}{\left|v-w\right|^{d+2s}}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{2}\int_{[t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big(f_{\delta}^{2}\big)\phi^{2}\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle\leq CR^{-2s}\int_{[t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}f_{\delta}^{2}\,{\rm d}z.

In particular,
C_{d,s,\lambda}
and
C
are uniform constants in
\delta
, thus
f_{\delta}\in L^{2}((t_{1},t_{2})\times B_{R^{1+2s}};H^{s}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))
uniformly in
\delta
.

Moreover, we observe that
f_{\delta}
satisfies

\mathcal{T}f_{\delta}=(-\Delta_{v})^{s}f_{\delta}+\int_{\left|v-w\right|>\delta}\big(f_{\delta}(w)-f_{\delta}(v)\big)\big(1-\eta_{\delta}(v-w)\big)\big(K(v,w)-c_{d,s}\left|v-w\right|^{-(d+2s)}\big)\,{\rm d}w

in
[t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
. This is the Kolmogorov equation with bounded right hand side

h_{\delta}(t,x,v):=\int_{\left|v-w\right|>\delta}\big(f_{\delta}(w)-f_{\delta}(v)\big)\big(1-\eta_{\delta}(v-w)\big)\big(K(v,w)-c_{d,s}\left|v-w\right|^{-(d+2s)}\big)\,{\rm d}w.

Thus
f_{\delta}
is given by

f_{\delta}(t,x,v)=h_{\delta}\ast J(t,x,v)+f_{\delta}(t_{1},x,v)\ast J(t_{1},x,v),

where
J
is the fundamental solution of the Kolmogorov equation in
[t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.
We find on the one hand, for any
2\leq p<\frac{2d(s+1)+2s}{d(s+1)}
, if we let
\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}
, then by Young’s inequality, the scaling of the fundamental solution (
47
) and Theorem
7

\displaystyle\left\|f_{\delta}\right\|_{L^{p}([t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|(-\Delta_{v})^{-\frac{s}{2}}h_{\delta}\right\|_{L^{2}([t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\left\|(-\Delta_{v})^{\frac{s}{2}}J\right\|_{L^{q}([t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq CR^{-s}\left\|f_{\delta}\right\|_{L^{2}([t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}};\dot{H}^{s}_{v}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))}

\displaystyle\leq CR^{-2s}\left\|f_{\delta}\right\|_{L^{2}([t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}))},

uniformly in
\delta
.

On the other hand, since
\left\|h_{\delta}\right\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\delta^{-2s}\left\|f_{\delta}\right\|_{L^{2}}
, we find, due to (
47
),
f_{\delta}\in L^{1}([t_{1},t_{2}]);L^{2}(B_{R^{1+2s}});H^{\frac{3s}{2}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))
with a constant that degenerates as
\delta\to 0
. This follows by using Young’s inequality for convolutions:

\displaystyle\left\|f_{\delta}\right\|_{L^{1}([t_{1},t_{2}]);L^{2}(B_{R^{1+2s}});\dot{H}^{\frac{3s}{2}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|h_{\delta}\ast J\right\|_{L^{1}([t_{1},t_{2}]);L^{2}(B_{R^{1+2s}});\dot{H}^{\frac{3s}{2}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))}+\left\|f_{\delta}(t_{1})\ast J\right\|_{L^{1}([t_{1},t_{2}]);L^{2}(B_{R^{1+2s}});\dot{H}^{\frac{3s}{2}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|h_{\delta}\ast(-\Delta_{v})^{\frac{3s}{4}}J\right\|_{L^{1}([t_{1},t_{2}]);L^{2}(B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}+\left\|f_{\delta}(t_{1})\ast(-\Delta_{v})^{\frac{3s}{4}}J\right\|_{L^{1}([t_{1},t_{2}]);L^{2}(B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|h_{\delta}\ast t^{-\frac{3}{4}}\right\|_{L^{1}([t_{1},t_{2}]);L^{2}(B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}+C\left\|f_{\delta}(t_{1})\right\|_{L^{2}(B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|h_{\delta}\right\|_{L^{2}([t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}+C\left\|f_{\delta}(t_{1})\right\|_{L^{2}(B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq C\delta^{-2s}\left\|f_{\delta}\right\|_{L^{2}([t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}+C\left\|f_{\delta}(t_{1})\right\|_{L^{2}(B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

Thus we can use any test function in
L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}H_{v}^{\frac{s}{2}}
to give sense to the equation; in particular
\psi_{l}
constructed in (
32
) is admissible. We deduce the a priori estimate on
f_{\delta}
, but since the
\dot{H}_{v}^{\frac{3s}{2}}
norm of
f_{\delta}
is not quantitatively affecting the estimate of Proposition
11
, the final estimate is uniform in
\delta
.
We find:

(102)

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\frac{3R}{4}}}

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{R}}(f_{\delta}-l)_{+}(w)\chi_{f_{\delta}>l}(v)K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq CR_{0}^{n(1-\zeta)-2s}\mathcal{M}_{l}\left|\big\{f_{\delta}>l\big\}\cap Q_{R_{0}}\right|^{\zeta},

where
C
does not depend on
\delta
.
Now we want to pass to the limit. Note that as
\delta\to 0
, the coefficients
K_{\delta}
converge to
K
pointwise a.e. Due to
[
35
, Lemma 4.1]
and
[
22
, Theorem 1.1]
(which both rely merely on the
L^{2}_{t,x}H^{s}_{v}
norm of
f_{\delta}
) we know
f_{\delta}\in L^{\infty}_{loc}([t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
uniformly in
\delta
, and moreover
f_{\delta}\in C^{\alpha}_{loc}([t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
uniformly in
\delta
for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
. As a consequence of the uniform estimates,
f_{\delta}
converges to some
f:[t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
pointwise almost everywhere in
[t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, weakly in
L^{2}([t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}};H^{s}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))
by weak compactness, and strongly in
L^{1}([t_{1},t_{2}]\times B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
by uniform integrability, pointwise convergence and tightness. In particular,
f\geq 0
and it satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\frac{3R}{4}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\setminus B_{R}}(f-l)_{+}(w)\chi_{f>l}(v)K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v\leq CR_{0}^{n(1-\zeta)-2s}\mathcal{M}_{l}\left|\big\{f>l\big\}\cap Q_{R_{0}}\right|^{\zeta}.

It remains to check that
f
satisfies the limit equation

\mathcal{T}f=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big(f(w)-f(v)\big)K(v,w)\,{\rm d}w.

Let
\varphi\in L^{2}((t_{1},t_{2})\times B_{R^{1+2s}},H^{s}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))
such that
\varphi(\cdot,\cdot,v)
has compact support in
(t_{1},t_{2})\times B_{R^{1+2s}}
. Then
f_{\delta}
satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{(t_{1},t_{2})\times B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}(\mathcal{T}f_{\delta})\varphi\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle+\int_{(t_{1},t_{2})}\int_{B_{R^{1+2s}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big(f_{\delta}(v)-f_{\delta}(w)\big)K_{\delta}(v,w)\varphi(v)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t=0.

Integrating by parts and using the global convergence of the coefficients pointwise and strongly in
L^{1}
, and by weak compactness in
L^{2}_{t,x}H^{s}_{v}
, we see that
f
satisfies

\displaystyle-\int_{(t_{1},t_{2})\times B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}f\mathcal{T}\varphi\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle+\int_{(t_{1},t_{2})}\int_{B_{R^{1+2s}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}f(v)K(v,w)(\varphi(v)-\varphi(w)\big)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t=0,

that is

\displaystyle\int_{(t_{1},t_{2})\times B_{R^{1+2s}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}(\mathcal{T}f)\varphi\,{\rm d}z

\displaystyle+\int_{(t_{1},t_{2})}\int_{B_{R^{1+2s}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big(f(v)-f(w)\big)K(v,w)\varphi(v)\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}t=0.

Appendix B
Upper bound in the parabolic case

In this subsection, we discuss how the method outlined above to derive the polynomial decay of Theorem
2
can be improved for kernels that satisfy a pointwise upper bound (
13
) in case that we are in the parabolic setting:
J
is constant in
x
. The most significant sub-optimality stems from the way we treated the non-singular part in Aronson’s method in Proposition
17
. For pointwisely bouded kernels, instead of (
78
), we obtain:

(103)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(\tau_{0},\sigma)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f^{2}(t,x,v)H(t,x,v)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v\leq

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f^{2}(\tau_{0},x,v)H(\tau_{0},x,v)\,{\rm d}x\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle+C\rho^{-(d+2s)}\left\|H\right\|_{L^{\infty}([\tau_{0},\sigma]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}([\tau_{0},\sigma];L^{1}_{v}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))}^{2}.

This follows by replacing the estimate on the non-singular part of (
83
) by

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{loc}^{ns}

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{2R}}\int_{B_{2R}}\big[f(w)-f(v)\big]\Big[\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(v)-\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(w)\Big]K(v,w)\chi_{\left|v-w\right|>\rho}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{2R}}\int_{B_{2R}}\Big[f(w)\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(v)+f(v)\big(fH\varphi_{R}^{2}\big)(w)\Big]K(v,w)\chi_{\left|v-w\right|>\rho}\,{\rm d}w\,{\rm d}v

\displaystyle\leq C\rho^{-2s-d}\left\|H\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{B_{2R}}f(w)\,{\rm d}w\int_{B_{2R}}f(v)\,{\rm d}v.

The construction of
H
remains the same. Therefore, this improvement leads in the proof of Theorem
16
to the replacement of (
94
) by

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(\tau_{0},\sigma)}\int_{B^{0}_{2\rho}(t,w_{0})}f^{2}(t,v)\,{\rm d}v\leq\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{1-\frac{\gamma}{3\rho}}\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{2}+\rho^{-(d+2s)}(\sigma-\tau_{0})\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{2},

which subsequently implies an improved estimate in Theorem
16
:

(104)

\displaystyle\left|f(\sigma,w_{0})\right|^{2}\leq C(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{d}{2s}}\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{1-\frac{\gamma}{3\rho}}\left\|f_{0}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})}+\rho^{-(d+2s)}(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{1-\frac{d}{2s}}\left\|f_{0}\right\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{2}.

Using (
104
) in the proof of Theorem
15
, yields the following improved estimate in Theorem
15
:

J(\sigma,v;\tau_{0},w_{0})\leq C\Bigg((\sigma-\tau_{0})^{-\frac{d}{2s}}\Bigg(\frac{\rho^{2s}}{k(\sigma-\tau_{0})}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\gamma}{12\rho}}+\rho^{-\frac{d+2s}{4}}(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{\frac{2s-3d}{8s}}\Bigg),

so that eventually, choosing
\rho
as in (
95
), we conclude for
(\sigma-\tau_{0})\leq\frac{\rho^{2s}}{4}

\displaystyle J(\sigma,v;\tau_{0},w_{0})\lesssim

\displaystyle(\sigma-\tau_{0})\left|v-w_{0}\right|^{-(d+2s)}+(\sigma-\tau_{0})^{\frac{2s-3d}{8s}}\left|v-w_{0}\right|^{-\frac{d+2s}{4}}.

Note that in the parabolic case, the optimal decay in velocity is
d+2s
, which suggests that our method - which does not rely on Meyer’s decomposition - is sub-optimal by a factor of
\frac{1}{4}
.
